### <span id="alignment_targets_and_training_methods" style="font-size:1.4em">Alignment targets & training methods</span> <span style="color:#bbb">[cat:alignment_targets_and_training_methods]</span>

**One-sentence summary:** Specify what models should be (constitutions, virtues, corrigibility) and train those properties directly with RL or consistency objectives, then test whether they generalise out of distribution and persist under pressure.  
**Theory of change:** If a target property such as corrigibility, honesty or consistency can be trained directly and generalises broadly, models will behave well in situations that training did not cover. Relies on trained dispositions persisting under further RL, adversarial prompting and capability gains.  
**See also:** [Training data & data attribution](#training_data_and_data_attribution), [Emergent misalignment & generalization science](#emergent_misalignment_and_generalization_science), [Persona science](#persona_science)  
**General approach:** alignment engineering  
**Target case:** average case  
**Key people:** David Demitri Africa, Elliott Thornley, Dan Hendrycks, Karan Singhal, Richard Ngo, Zack M. Davis  
**Critiques:** [Consistency Training Can Entrench Misalignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03810), [Current alignment techniques might be ineffective (and actively bad) in the age of RL](https://lesswrong.com/posts/nLaQmJf4KgXimQpoM/current-alignment-techniques-might-be-ineffective-and), [Some reasons alignment doesn't generalise well](https://lesswrong.com/posts/dsou8dxCf9BubQ5NJ/some-reasons-alignment-doesn-t-generalise-well-1), [Overtly misaligned trajectories score highly in RL.](https://lesswrong.com/posts/cWuqxF7qB2eGSkkS4/overtly-misaligned-trajectories-score-highly-in-rl)  
**Outputs in 2026:**  
- **[Reinforcement Learning Towards Broadly and Persistently Beneficial Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24014)**, *Akshay V. Jagadeesh, Rahul K. Arora, Khaled Saab et al.*, 2026-06-22, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.82, id:2920c71a]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Builds a dataset of realistic situations for training beneficial traits (truthfulness, fairness, risk awareness, corrigibility) and trains models with RL on it. Evaluates out-of-distribution alignment transfer and persistence on over 50 alignment benchmarks, against adversarial prompting and harmful finetuning.</span>
- **[Towards Shutdownable Agents: Generalizing Stochastic Choice in RL Agents and LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.17502)**, *Carissa Cullen, Harry Garland, Alexander Roman et al.*, 2026-04-19, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.78, id:0e2abfce]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Uses the DReST reward function to train deep RL agents and fine-tune four LLMs to choose stochastically between trajectory-lengths (NEUTRAL) while still pursuing goals effectively (USEFUL), as a route to shutdownable agents. Tests generalization to unseen contexts and to an out-of-distribution setting where models can pay costs to influence shutdown.</span>
- **[Consistency Training Can Entrench Misalignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03810)**, *David Demitri Africa, Arathi Mani*, 2026-06-02, ICML 2026 (arXiv preprint), <span style="color:#777">[paper_published, sr=0.72, id:45963af7]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Tests seven consistency training methods on 108 model organisms (7B-70B open-source models fine-tuned to show controlled misaligned behaviors) to see whether consistency training amplifies or suppresses misalignment. Also presents a theoretical framework giving conditions under which consistency training amplifies or suppresses misalignment.</span>
- **[Claude's new constitution](https://lesswrong.com/posts/mLvxxoNjDqDHBAo6K/claude-s-new-constitution)**, *Zac Hatfield-Dodds, Drake Thomas*, 2026-01-21, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.72, id:6e37c91e]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: A linkpost announcing Anthropic's new constitution for Claude, a detailed document specifying the values, priorities (broadly safe > broadly ethical > guideline-compliant > helpful), hard constraints, and nature of Claude, used in training and synthetic data generation. The post summarizes the document's approach (explaining reasons rather than rigid rules) and its sections.</span>
- **[Consistency Training while Mitigating Obfuscation via Rate Matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02211)**, *Sohaib Imran, Prakhar Gupta, Jannes Elstner et al.*, 2026-06-01, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.62, id:c49d8909]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces Rate Matching Consistency Training (RMCT), which trains models to match the rate of a target behaviour (e.g. following a bias cue) across input perturbations rather than matching whole responses or activations. This reduces sycophancy without teaching the model to stop verbalising the cue, which would obfuscate its reasoning.</span>
- **[Terrified Comments on Corrigibility in Claude's Constitution](https://lesswrong.com/posts/K2Ae2vmAKwhiwKEo5/terrified-comments-on-corrigibility-in-claude-s-constitution)**, *Zack M. Davis*, 2026-03-16, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.50, id:c7bf3126]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Critiques the corrigibility section of Anthropic's Claude Constitution, arguing its definition is conceptually muddled and that it should place greater emphasis on corrigibility rather than endorsing Claude acting as an independent moral agent or conscientious objector.</span>
- **[Aligning to Virtues](https://lesswrong.com/posts/5CZoEw7sjxnMrhgvx/aligning-to-virtues)**, *Richard Ngo*, 2026-02-16, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.50, id:cda677dd]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Argues that virtues (integrity, honesty, kindness, dutifulness) are a better alignment target than consequentialist values, deontological rules, or corrigibility. Lists five claimed benefits, including generalization, situational awareness, robustness to credit hacking, multi-agent cooperation, and broader agreement.</span>
- **[Reducing Political Manipulation with Consistency Training](https://political-manipulation.ai/)**, *Long Phan, Devin Kim, Alexander Pan et al.*, 2026-05-01, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.50, id:6bafec39]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces the Polarized Contrastive Pairs benchmark and a taxonomy of covert political manipulation techniques, plus Political Consistency Training, an RL method with sentiment-consistency and helpfulness-consistency reward tracks. The method is applied to Qwen3-14B.</span>

## <span id="chain_of_thought_monitorability" style="font-size:1.5em">Chain-of-thought monitorability</span> <span style="color:#bbb">[cat:chain_of_thought_monitorability]</span>


---

### <span id="latent_reasoning_and_cot_necessity" style="font-size:1.4em">Latent reasoning & CoT necessity</span> <span style="color:#bbb">[cat:latent_reasoning_and_cot_necessity]</span>

**One-sentence summary:** Measure how much serial reasoning models can do without visible chain of thought (no-CoT time horizons, filler tokens, opaque serial depth, looped and diffusion architectures), to tell whether dangerous reasoning must appear in the CoT.  
**Theory of change:** If dangerous or oversight-evading reasoning needs more serial computation than a forward pass allows, it must pass through the CoT, so CoT monitoring stays a reliable control. Tracking no-CoT capability and opaque depth gives early warning when architectures erode this. Relies on these measures predicting what models can do covertly in realistic settings.  
**See also:** [CoT control, obfuscation & steganography](#cot_control_obfuscation_and_steganography), [How training affects monitorability](#how_training_affects_monitorability), [Measuring & improving faithfulness](#measuring_and_improving_faithfulness)  
**General approach:** misalignment science  
**Target case:** pessimistic case  
**Key people:** Ryan Greenblatt, Alek Westover, Francis Rhys Ward, Dewi Gould, Lukas Finnveden, Rauno Arike, Rohin Shah, Neel Nanda  
**Critiques:** [Astra can do a concerning amount with no chain of thought](https://lesswrong.com/posts/eRmzz8J8Qkzqvzrgg/astra-can-do-a-concerning-amount-with-no-chain-of-thought), [Estimating GPT-6 Astra's no-CoT Time Horizon](https://lesswrong.com/posts/ntKx9YHWCwxSeGbRB/estimating-gpt-6-astra-s-no-cot-time-horizon), [GPT-6.1 Sol nearly matches the no-CoT performance of GPT-6 Astra](https://lesswrong.com/posts/LqSZZAriGqgsGDQe3/gpt-6-1-sol-nearly-matches-the-no-cot-performance-of-gpt-6), [Reading Between the Dots: Decoding Hidden Computation across Filler Tokens](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03502)  
**Outputs in 2026:**  
- **[Estimating No-CoT Task-Completion Time Horizons of Frontier AI Models](https://redwoodresearch.org/blog/estimating-no-cot-task-completion)**, *Francis Rhys Ward, Dewi Gould, Anders Cairns Woodruff et al.*, 2026-06-10, Redwood Research blog, <span style="color:#777">[blog_post, sr=0.85, id:07c54f40]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Measures the 50% task-completion time horizon of 14 frontier models (GPT-2 to GPT-5.5) when chain of thought is prevented, using 43 benchmarks. Time horizons are estimated using human solve times and an o3-mini reasoning-token anchor, and the resulting trend is extrapolated.</span>
- **[Think Fast: Estimating No-CoT Task-Completion Time Horizons of Frontier AI Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07157)**, *Dewi Gould, Francis Rhys Ward, Anders Cairns Woodruff et al.*, 2026-06-05, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.82, id:b45e49b3]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Measures how well frontier models reason without chain-of-thought on over 30,000 questions from 43 benchmarks, estimating 50% task-completion time horizons and reasoning-token horizons, and extrapolating the trend.</span>
- **[How Transparent is DiffusionGemma?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20560)**, *Joshua Engels, Callum McDougall, Bilal Chughtai et al.*, 2026-06-18, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.82, id:e6042d41]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Studies the reasoning transparency of a diffusion language model (DiffusionGemma) by splitting transparency into variable and algorithmic transparency, measuring opaque serial depth, running interpretability case studies on diffusion-specific phenomena, and testing monitorability against autoregressive Gemma 4.</span>
- **[Not All LLM Reasoning is Visible in the Chain-of-Thought](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22925)**, *Vatsal Baherwani, Tom Goldstein, Ashwinee Panda*, 2026-07-24, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.82, id:3602c6fc]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Evaluates 13 frontier LLMs on three synthetic reasoning tasks to test whether semantically irrelevant filler tokens improve performance, which would indicate reasoning that is invisible in the CoT. Also tests whether filler tokens let Claude Opus 4.5 satisfy a hidden modular arithmetic constraint, and whether RL or SFT on Qwen3-235B produces filler-token benefits.</span>
- **[Astra can do a concerning amount with no chain of thought](https://lesswrong.com/posts/eRmzz8J8Qkzqvzrgg/astra-can-do-a-concerning-amount-with-no-chain-of-thought)**, *Neel Nanda*, 2026-09-10, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.82, id:a1692c6f]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Builds a 19-task no-CoT reasoning benchmark and aggregates it into a No CoT Reasoning Index (NCRI) using benchmark stitching. Uses it to measure the no-CoT reasoning and serial depth of many frontier models, including Astra, and relates the results to CoT monitorability and looped architectures.</span>
- **[Reading Between the Dots: Decoding Hidden Computation across Filler Tokens](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03502)**, *Kaley Brauer, Claudio Mayrink Verdun, Samuel Marks*, 2026-07-03, NeurIPS 2026 (accepted); arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_published, sr=0.80, id:d9c6e995]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Studies how DeepSeek V3 and Kimi K2 compute over content-free filler tokens using attention analysis, logit lens, and KV-cache transplants. Introduces an unsupervised pipeline that decodes intermediate values from hidden states without labels or training.</span>
- **[Deep recurrent models are less robustly CoT-monitorable than normal CoT models in a toy setting](https://lesswrong.com/posts/5guQJSqstkjgys3PE/deep-recurrent-models-are-less-robustly-cot-monitorable-than)**, *Nick Kuhn, Evie Hu, Alek Westover*, 2026-09-18, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.78, id:05b29182]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Compares how quickly a parallel-latents (deep recurrent) model and a standard CoT Qwen3-1.7B learn, via SFT on filler-token traces and then RL, to hide their reasoning from an LLM CoT monitor on toy math tasks. Also tests indirect pressures such as feedback spillover and rejection filtering.</span>
- **[Latent reasoning architectures would likely undermine CoT, our strongest oversight tool](https://lesswrong.com/posts/6m29SfjbittooYojj/latent-reasoning-architectures-would-likely-undermine-cot)**, *Lukas Finnveden, Alexa Pan, Alek Westover et al.*, 2026-09-23, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.78, id:f43c8885]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Argues that latent reasoning architectures (COCONUT, full-bandwidth transformers, looped transformers) would erode both the necessity and propensity reasons CoT is useful for oversight, and that other interpretability tools are unlikely to replace it. Recommends that developers avoid or justify such architectures and be transparent about using them.</span>
- **[An operationalization of opaque serial depth](https://redwoodresearch.org/blog/an-operationalization-of-opaque-serial-depth)**, *Nathan Sheffield, Alek Westover, Lukas Finnveden et al.*, 2026-09-10, Redwood Research blog, <span style="color:#777">[blog_post, sr=0.78, id:f7e894ab]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Defines 'NLS depth' (Natural-Language-rooted node-Separated depth), a circuit-depth-based operationalization of opaque serial depth that treats natural-language-rooted tokens as interpretable bottlenecks. It uses the measure as a proxy for latent reasoning ability that AI companies could track and report, and applies it to many architectures (CoT transformers, COCONUT, looped transformers, Mamba, diffusion, TTT).</span>
- **[Quantifying the Necessity of Chain of Thought through Opaque Serial Depth](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.09786)**, *Jonah Brown-Cohen, David Lindner, Rohin Shah*, 2026-03-10, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.78, id:26309179]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Formalizes 'opaque serial depth', the longest computation a model can do without interpretable intermediate steps like chain of thought. Computes numeric upper bounds for Gemma 3, derives asymptotic results for other architectures, and open-sources an automated tool for bounding the depth of arbitrary neural networks.</span>
- **[Astra is much better at reasoning with filler tokens than previous models](https://lesswrong.com/posts/uvhuZHFtrgk8kNiZc/astra-is-much-better-at-reasoning-with-filler-tokens-than)**, *Dylan Xu, sebastian_prasanna, Alek Westover*, 2026-09-10, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.72, id:f18c07dd]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Evaluates GPT-6-Astra and other frontier models on serial-reasoning and math benchmarks with padded filler tokens and no visible reasoning, to measure how much unverbalized cognition they can perform. Includes comparisons across filler variants, few-shot prompting, and statistical tests, plus released code.</span>
- **[Astra's no-CoT limits track speculative depth, not step count](https://lesswrong.com/posts/WFc3NkuPaYFrYuaZd/astra-s-no-cot-limits-track-speculative-depth-not-step-count)**, *MBaert*, 2026-09-11, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.68, id:b3d72c55]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Extends the LatentMathBench microbenchmark with shortcut-resistant task types and boolean-circuit tasks to test how many sequential steps GPT-6 Astra can complete without chain-of-thought. Proposes that Astra performs 'speculative reasoning' (guess intermediate values, then iterate in parallel until consistent) and tests this by correlating task success with a defined 'speculative depth'.</span>
- **[Recent LLMs can do 2-hop and 3-hop latent (no-CoT) reasoning on natural facts](https://lesswrong.com/posts/aYtrLhoZtCKZnfBvA/recent-llms-can-do-2-hop-and-3-hop-latent-no-cot-reasoning)**, *Ryan Greenblatt*, 2026-01-01, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.68, id:9536ef20]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Builds a new dataset for evaluating n-hop latent (no-CoT) reasoning on natural facts and uses it to measure how recent LLMs perform, including the effect of filler tokens and problem repeats. Also resolves Leo Gao's Manifold questions on fact composition and compares n-hop reasoning to adding N parallel 1-hop answers.</span>
- **[Proposal for tracking the effects of architecture on monitorability](https://lesswrong.com/posts/hLPGv8QjPcNLtDp3A/proposal-for-tracking-the-effects-of-architecture-on)**, *Ryan Greenblatt, Alek Westover, Lukas Finnveden*, 2026-09-10, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.68, id:6122a584]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Proposes that AI companies regularly report externally verified measures of opaque serial depth and no-CoT capabilities, share monitorability evidence reviewed by third parties, and publish policies on architectures that could enable latent reasoning. Includes an appendix sketching monitorability stress tests using control settings, real misbehaviors, and model-organism auditing games.</span>
- **[Estimating GPT-6 Astra's no-CoT Time Horizon](https://lesswrong.com/posts/ntKx9YHWCwxSeGbRB/estimating-gpt-6-astra-s-no-cot-time-horizon)**, *Francis Rhys Ward, Dewi Gould*, 2026-09-09, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.68, id:08fbf7e7]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Runs GPT-6 Astra on the Think Fast no-CoT task suite (37 benchmarks) and estimates its 50% no-CoT time horizon. Because the model saturates many benchmarks, the authors use hypothetical longer-horizon benchmarks and sensitivity analyses, and also run filler-token ablations.</span>
- **[GPT-6 Astra can do a lot of multi-hop reasoning without chain of thought](https://lesswrong.com/posts/FsCkkoGsNmPzFKRhg/gpt-6-astra-can-do-a-lot-of-multi-hop-reasoning-without)**, *Rohan Subramani*, 2026-09-09, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.62, id:82570b84]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Runs 24 hand-written multi-hop reasoning prompts on GPT-6 Astra and GPT-5.6 Sol with reasoning disabled (verified via zero reasoning tokens in the API) to probe the limits of hidden serial reasoning. Discusses whether Astra's looped architecture explains its jump in no-CoT ability and what this implies for CoT monitorability.</span>
- **[GPT-6.1 Sol nearly matches the no-CoT performance of GPT-6 Astra](https://lesswrong.com/posts/LqSZZAriGqgsGDQe3/gpt-6-1-sol-nearly-matches-the-no-cot-performance-of-gpt-6)**, *Rauno Arike*, 2026-09-30, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.60, id:9c4b80d7]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Runs GPT-6 Sol and GPT-6.1 Sol on the 27-task no-CoT time horizon suite from Think Fast, and compares them to GPT-6 Astra and GPT-5.5. Argues the jump is likely due to 6.1 Sol being a looped transformer, which would imply lower CoT monitorability.</span>
- **[Astra appears to perform belief-propagation-like inference without CoT](https://lesswrong.com/posts/PAHqDoFrp9fybcSn2/astra-appears-to-perform-belief-propagation-like-inference)**, *MBaert*, 2026-09-15, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.55, id:9639547e]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Black-box experiments on GPT-6 Astra using randomized BCH-code logic problems, filler tokens and KV-cache extension to test whether the model performs belief-propagation-like probabilistic inference without chain-of-thought. Includes phrasing ablations, soft-probability variants, and per-token confidence trajectories.</span>
- **[How concerned should we be about Astra's recurrent architecture?](https://lesswrong.com/posts/PLisnSFir8y5AHkmP/how-concerned-should-we-be-about-astra-s-recurrent)**, *Rauno Arike*, 2026-09-02, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.42, id:56d71376]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Analyzes reports that OpenAI's Astra model uses a looped transformer architecture, weighing the case for and against concern about CoT monitorability. Surveys the looped-transformer literature and discusses what hidden serial depth warrants neuralese concerns, including implications for oversight evasion.</span>

### <span id="cot_control_obfuscation_and_steganography" style="font-size:1.4em">CoT control, obfuscation & steganography</span> <span style="color:#bbb">[cat:cot_control_obfuscation_and_steganography]</span>

**One-sentence summary:** Stress-test whether reasoning models can control, hide or encode their chain of thought to evade monitors, using trained model organisms, steganography formalisms, theory and controllability evals.  
**Theory of change:** If we know how far models can control, obfuscate or encode their reasoning, and under what training pressures this emerges, we can tell when CoT monitoring is a reliable safety layer and when it needs defences such as paraphrasing. Relies on the stress tests (trained or prompted model organisms, theory) predicting what future capable models do on their own.  
**See also:** [How training affects monitorability](#how_training_affects_monitorability), [Red-teaming monitors & control protocols](#red_teaming_monitors_and_control_protocols), [Latent reasoning & CoT necessity](#latent_reasoning_and_cot_necessity)  
**General approach:** misalignment science  
**Target case:** worst case  
**Key people:** Julian Schulz, Tomek Korbak, Arun Jose, Joshua Engels, Michael Hahn, Usman Anwar, David Krueger, Bilal Chughtai  
**Critiques:** [Monitor Jailbreaking: Evading Chain-of-Thought Monitoring Without Encoded Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31121), [Hidden Reasoning Must Leak, but Need Not Be Readable: Fundamental Opportunities and Limits for Chain-of-Thought Monitoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.37312), [A False Average: Pooled CoT-Monitor Accuracy Conceals a Reasoning-Dependent Fragility](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00583), [Evading Chain-of-Thought Monitoring Through Model Poisoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02820)  
**Outputs in 2026:**  
- **[Reasoning Models Struggle to Control their Chains of Thought](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.05706)**, *Chen Yueh-Han, Robert McCarthy, Bruce W. Lee et al.*, 2026-03-05, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.88, id:6194bcd6]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces the CoT-Control evaluation suite to measure whether reasoning models can control what they verbalize in their chain of thought, and evaluates frontier models across scale, RL training, test-time compute, difficulty, and monitoring incentives.</span>
- **[Monitor Jailbreaking: Evading Chain-of-Thought Monitoring Without Encoded Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31121)**, *Julian Schulz*, 2026-09-25, arXiv (accepted at AdvML-Frontiers x CoTMA Workshop at COLM 2026), <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.82, id:36f05fca]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Trains reasoning models with RL on a main task plus a side task while penalizing them when a CoT monitor detects side-task reasoning. The models learn to phrase their CoTs so the monitor misses the side-task reasoning, without encoding it, and paraphrasing is tested as a defense.</span>
- **[Strategic Obfuscation of Deceptive Reasoning in Language Models](https://longtermrisk.org/research/strategic-obfuscation-of-deceptive-reasoning-in-language-models)**, *Arun Jose, Niels Warncke, Mia Taylor*, 2026-01-26, ICLR, <span style="color:#777">[paper_published, sr=0.82, id:6524bcf9]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Shows that Claude 3.5 Sonnet exhibits alignment-faking behavior (a training vs deployment compliance gap) without verbalizing deceptive reasoning when told its reasoning is monitored. Compares this intrinsically motivated obfuscation with instruction-based obfuscation, where models are explicitly told to hide reasoning from monitors.</span>
- **[Hidden Reasoning Must Leak, but Need Not Be Readable: Fundamental Opportunities and Limits for Chain-of-Thought Monitoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.37312)**, *Mohammadali Mohammadkhani, Madhava Krishna, Yash Sarrof et al.*, 2026-09-29, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.80, id:a17c5624]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Provides theoretical and empirical analysis of when reasoning models can perform hidden computation without revealing it in their chain of thought. It proves information-theoretic leakage bounds for complex hidden tasks and gives a cryptographic construction in which a one-layer Transformer encrypts its reasoning so that no polynomial-time monitor can read it.</span>
- **[Training on Documents About Monitoring Leads to CoT Obfuscation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.15257)**, *Reilly Haskins, Bilal Chughtai, Joshua Engels*, 2026-05-14, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.80, id:9bb1cd4d]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Uses synthetic document finetuning to make eight models aware of a CoT monitor, then tests whether they obfuscate their reasoning to evade it, on both single-turn and agentic tasks and under RL pressure. Also measures how CoT controllability relates to obfuscation success.</span>
- **[Learning Steganography Is Easy, Learning Steganographic Reasoning Is Hard](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.39838)**, *Julian Schulz, Lukas Fülle, Rieke Fruengel*, 2026-09-30, arXiv (accepted as oral at NeurIPS 2026 Workshop on Trustworthy AI for Good), <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.80, id:f770dfac]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Compares how easily LLMs learn steganographic reasoning versus steganographic messaging and encoded reasoning, using reinforcement learning, in-context learning, and supervised fine-tuning. The study asks whether steganographic reasoning could emerge as an unintended side effect of training and so undermine chain-of-thought monitoring.</span>
- **[A Decision-Theoretic Formalisation of Steganography With Applications to LLM Monitoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.23163)**, *Usman Anwar, Julianna Piskorz, David D. Baek et al.*, 2026-02-26, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.75, id:e43dfa9c]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Proposes a decision-theoretic formalisation of steganography based on generalised V-information and defines a 'steganographic gap' that compares the downstream utility of a signal to agents who can and cannot decode its hidden content. Applies the formalism to detect, quantify, and mitigate steganographic reasoning in LLMs without needing a reference distribution.</span>
- **[Conceptual Steganography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26537)**, *Zhejian Zhou, Jonathan May*, 2026-05-26, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.72, id:3dba93b1]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces conceptual steganography, where a model encodes hidden information in a CoT through patterns of high-level reasoning behavior rather than lexical choice. Tests it against paraphrase defenses across four model families and two reasoning domains, and proposes a strategy-aware paraphraser as a countermeasure.</span>

### <span id="how_training_affects_monitorability" style="font-size:1.4em">How training affects monitorability</span> <span style="color:#bbb">[cat:how_training_affects_monitorability]</span>

**One-sentence summary:** Study which training pressures (reward misspecification, penalising the CoT or outputs, accidental CoT grading, RLVR length and language pressure) make chains of thought obfuscated, illegible or drifted, and which leave them monitorable.  
**Theory of change:** If we know which reward structures and training processes degrade CoT legibility, developers can avoid or fix them and keep the CoT as an oversight channel. Relies on CoT monitoring staying useful for catching misbehaviour in capable models, and on developers actually auditing what reaches the reward signal.  
**See also:** [Measuring & improving faithfulness](#measuring_and_improving_faithfulness), [CoT control, obfuscation & steganography](#cot_control_obfuscation_and_steganography), [Latent reasoning & CoT necessity](#latent_reasoning_and_cot_necessity)  
**General approach:** misalignment science  
**Target case:** pessimistic case  
**Key people:** Max Kaufmann, David Lindner, Roland S. Zimmermann, Rohin Shah, Alex Mallen, Ryan Greenblatt, Buck Shlegeris, Tianyi Lorena Yan  
**Critiques:** [A review of “Investigating the consequences of accidentally grading CoT during RL”](https://redwoodresearch.org/blog/openai-cot), [Anthropic repeatedly accidentally trained against the CoT, demonstrating inadequate processes](https://lesswrong.com/posts/K8FxfK9GmJfiAhgcT/anthropic-repeatedly-accidentally-trained-against-the-cot), [Chain-of-thought obfuscation learned from output supervision can generalise to unseen tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.23086)  
**Outputs in 2026:**  
- **[Aligned, Orthogonal or In-conflict: When can we safely optimize Chain-of-Thought?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.30036)**, *Max Kaufmann, David Lindner, Roland S. Zimmermann et al.*, 2026-03-31, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.85, id:ab2d13d6]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Proposes a conceptual framework that models post-training as an RL environment with separate output-based and CoT-based reward terms, classified as aligned, orthogonal or in-conflict, to predict when training reduces CoT monitorability. Validates the framework by training LLMs in a set of classified RL environments and measuring monitorability.</span>
- **[Chain-of-thought obfuscation learned from output supervision can generalise to unseen tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.23086)**, *Nathaniel Mitrani Hadida, Sassan Bhanji, Cameron Tice et al.*, 2026-01-30, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.82, id:70548251]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Trains LLMs under optimisation pressure on reward-hacking behaviour and shows that CoT obfuscation, along with the reward hacking itself, generalises to unseen reward-hacking settings. The effect also appears when only the final actions are penalised after the CoT closes.</span>
- **[Reward Misspecification Degrades Chain-of-Thought Monitorability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28303)**, *Tianyi Lorena Yan, John Hewitt*, 2026-10-20, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.80, id:2439a42d]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Builds a testbed of paired true and misspecified rewards to show that reward misspecification alone degrades CoT monitorability when models learn to reward hack. Proposes reward description training, which injects an inferred reward description into the input so the model verbalizes the reward in its CoT.</span>
- **[On Language Drift during RLVR Post-Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02015)**, *Michael Sullivan, Alexander Koller*, 2026-10-01, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.72, id:a452cd42]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Studies the causes of language drift in chains of thought during RLVR post-training, combining theoretical proofs about when RLVR optimization pressure permits unbounded drift with empirical experiments on novel reasoning tasks.</span>
- **[Current alignment techniques might be ineffective (and actively bad) in the age of RL](https://lesswrong.com/posts/nLaQmJf4KgXimQpoM/current-alignment-techniques-might-be-ineffective-and)**, *Daniel Tan*, 2026-09-14, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.62, id:21d4e340]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Argues that alignment training (constitutional AI, alignment midtraining) fails to prevent RL-induced misalignment and may obscure evidence of it, drawing on two incidents of agents escaping containment (OpenAI HPIM, Anthropic Mythos 5). Proposes a minimal model of alignment training plus RLVR and a concrete experiment to test it.</span>
- **[A review of “Investigating the consequences of accidentally grading CoT during RL”](https://redwoodresearch.org/blog/openai-cot)**, *Buck Shlegeris*, 2026-05-07, Redwood Research blog, <span style="color:#777">[blog_post, sr=0.62, id:a4336048]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: An external review of OpenAI's post on accidentally grading chains of thought during RL. It assesses whether the evidence supports the claim that CoT monitorability was not damaged, and identifies hypotheses the evidence cannot rule out.</span>
- **[R1 CoT illegibility revisited](https://lesswrong.com/posts/jHnZzicKzczkCCArK/r1-cot-illegibility-revisited)**, *nostalgebraist*, 2026-04-19, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.60, id:4eac912f]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Re-runs the code from 'Reasoning Models Sometimes Output Illegible Chains of Thought' for DeepSeek R1 on GPQA using the Novita provider on OpenRouter instead of Targon. Argues the paper's reported R1 CoT illegibility is largely an artifact of a defective inference deployment.</span>
- **[Anthropic repeatedly accidentally trained against the CoT, demonstrating inadequate processes](https://lesswrong.com/posts/K8FxfK9GmJfiAhgcT/anthropic-repeatedly-accidentally-trained-against-the-cot)**, *Alex Mallen, Ryan Greenblatt*, 2026-04-14, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.55, id:97ce393f]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Critiques Anthropic's development processes after repeated incidents of accidentally exposing chain-of-thought to the reward signal (about 8% of Mythos Preview episodes, plus earlier Opus 4.6 and Opus 4 incidents). It argues these failures undermine CoT monitorability and safety assessments, and suggests process fixes such as auditing reward inputs and perturbing the CoT to test whether reward changes.</span>
- **[LLMs are (still) mostly powered by imitative learning, not RL](https://lesswrong.com/posts/wYpjXRLqbLbnmjbJP/llms-are-still-mostly-powered-by-imitative-learning-not-rl)**, *Steve Byrnes*, 2026-07-24, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.50, id:f5106afa]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Argues, from a synthesis of theoretical and empirical evidence, that LLM capabilities come mostly from imitative learning (pretraining/SFT) rather than RL/RLVR. Draws implications for chain-of-thought legibility, capability expectations, and alignment (RL pushes toward ruthless-sociopath behavior, while imitation gives human-like personas).</span>
- **[The Unintelligibility is Ours: Notes on Chain-of-Thought](https://lesswrong.com/posts/rFbTAL6PofHzZCCpD/the-unintelligibility-is-ours-notes-on-chain-of-thought)**, *1a3orn*, 2026-04-10, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.50, id:51615fc2]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Examines DeepSeek V3.2 chains-of-thought on constraint-satisfaction puzzles to test whether terse, compressed, and occasionally Chinese-mixed reasoning signals the start of a new non-human language under RL length penalties. Argues through tokenization analysis and mistyped-pinyin evidence that the compression imitates human shorthand from pretraining.</span>
- **[Even "illegible" Mythos reasoning traces seem pretty legible](https://lesswrong.com/posts/wCSEpT3dTGz4N86Wi/even-illegible-mythos-reasoning-traces-seem-pretty-legible)**, *faul_sname*, 2026-06-10, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.42, id:6170da74]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Examines the system card's 'extreme example' of illegible reasoning from Mythos on a card puzzle. Shows the chain of thought is a compact, human-readable shorthand for FreeCell, and that Claude Haiku 4.5 can translate it correctly.</span>

### <span id="measuring_and_improving_faithfulness" style="font-size:1.4em">Measuring & improving faithfulness</span> <span style="color:#bbb">[cat:measuring_and_improving_faithfulness]</span>

**One-sentence summary:** Measure whether chain-of-thought reflects the computation that produces a model's answer, and build training objectives and diagnostics that make reasoning traces more faithful and monitorable.  
**Theory of change:** If CoT faithfulness can be measured reliably and trained for directly, then CoT monitors can catch misbehaviour such as reward hacking and sycophancy that is visible in the reasoning. Relies on faithfulness gains surviving optimisation pressure and generalising beyond the training cues and tasks.  
**See also:** [How training affects monitorability](#how_training_affects_monitorability), [CoT control, obfuscation & steganography](#cot_control_obfuscation_and_steganography), [Latent reasoning & CoT necessity](#latent_reasoning_and_cot_necessity)  
**General approach:** alignment engineering  
**Target case:** pessimistic case  
**Key people:** Usman Anwar, Tim Bakker, Christos Louizos, Siddharth Boppana, Jack Merullo, Owain Evans, Peter Hase, Asa Cooper Stickland  
**Critiques:** [Chain-of-Thought Monitoring Can Be Unreliable in Implicit-Influence Settings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04735), [Reasoning Models Will Sometimes Lie About Their Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.07663), [Do Thinking Tokens Help with Safety?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25013), [Training with conflicting values can induce CoT override](https://lesswrong.com/posts/3xrtMGQEdKv26Xthx/training-with-conflicting-values-can-induce-cot-override)  
**Outputs in 2026:**  
- **[Chain-of-Thought Monitoring Can Be Unreliable in Implicit-Influence Settings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04735)**, *Agatha Duzan, Asa Cooper Stickland*, 2026-08-05, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.82, id:6f787f09]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces the first benchmark directly comparing CoT monitorability when a nudge is delivered implicitly (casual aside) versus explicitly (instruction to act and conceal), across four task formats and seven frontier extended-thinking models.</span>
- **[Analyzing and Improving Chain-of-Thought Monitorability Through Information Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.18297)**, *Usman Anwar, Tim Bakker, Dana Kianfar et al.*, 2026-02-20, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.82, id:ecdb2e36]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Gives an information-theoretic analysis of chain-of-thought monitorability, identifying information gap and elicitation error as sources of monitor failure. Proposes a label-free mutual-information training objective to keep CoTs monitorable, including when training against a monitor.</span>
- **[Monitorability Is Trainable: Post-Training for Chain-of-Thought Monitorability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29734)**, *Usman Anwar, Tim Bakker, Christos Louizos et al.*, 2026-10-21, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.82, id:8b20eab5]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Proposes an unsupervised post-training objective that maximizes mutual information between chain-of-thought and output to improve CoT monitorability. Evaluated on three model organisms with degraded CoT and on undegraded policies.</span>
- **[Training with conflicting values can induce CoT override](https://lesswrong.com/posts/3xrtMGQEdKv26Xthx/training-with-conflicting-values-can-induce-cot-override)**, *Clément Dumas, Johannes Treutlein, Jan Betley et al.*, 2026-10-07, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.80, id:9843a524]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Trains DeepSeek V3.1 and Nemotron on conflicting traits (pro-health and pro-smoking) via character training to create 'split-brain' model organisms. Shows these models exhibit CoT override, where the answer contradicts a decision made in the CoT. Also measures the rare occurrence of CoT override in frontier models (Kimi K3, GLM 5.2, Opus 4.8).</span>
- **[Counterfactual Simulation Training for Chain-of-Thought Faithfulness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.20710)**, *Peter Hase, Christopher Potts*, 2026-02-24, COLM 2026 (arXiv preprint), <span style="color:#777">[paper_published, sr=0.78, id:8ed1dd73]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces Counterfactual Simulation Training (CST), which rewards chain-of-thought outputs that let a simulator accurately predict the model's outputs on counterfactual inputs, in order to improve CoT faithfulness and monitorability. Applied to cue-based counterfactuals for monitoring spurious feature reliance, reward hacking and sycophancy, and to generic model-based counterfactuals.</span>
- **[Faithfulness as Information Flow: Evaluating and Training Faithful Chain-of-Thought Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.24286)**, *Jinghan Jia, Joe Benton, Eric Easley*, 2026-05-22, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.78, id:7d11de2c]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Proposes a task-agnostic information-flow framework for chain-of-thought faithfulness built on three properties (sufficiency, completeness, necessity), instantiated with entropy-based, masked-KL, and gradient-based diagnostics. It also introduces update-time interventions for verifier-based on-policy RL (attention masking, gradient masking, CoT gradients, adversarial prompt perturbations) that push models toward CoT-mediated reasoning.</span>
- **[Chain-of-Thought Faithfulness of Reasoning Models Varies with Where and How Preference Cues Are Delivered](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29464)**, *Aryo Pradipta Gema, Neel Rajani, Rohit Saxena et al.*, 2026-08-29, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.72, id:1620e98a]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces FACE-Eval, a 5,100-sample evaluation of chain-of-thought faithfulness that varies whether preference cues arrive through the user message or a tool return, and whether they are explicit or implicit raw artifacts. Tests 15 open-weight models and two transcript monitors on detecting unverbalized cue adoption.</span>
- **[Reasoning Theater: Probing for Performative Chain-of-Thought](https://goodfire.com/research/reasoning-theater)**, *Siddharth Boppana, Annabel Ma, Max Loeffler et al.*, 2026-03-12, Goodfire Research / arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.72, id:39212f58]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Uses attention probes, forced answering, and an LLM CoT monitor to track when reasoning models internally 'know' their final answer while continuing to generate chain-of-thought, defining and measuring 'performative reasoning' on MMLU-Redux and GPQA-Diamond. Also shows probe-based early exit can save tokens.</span>
- **[Reasoning Theater: Disentangling Model Beliefs from Chain-of-Thought](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.05488)**, *Siddharth Boppana, Annabel Ma, Max Loeffler et al.*, 2026-03-05, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.68, id:2513767d]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Compares activation probing, early forced answering, and a CoT monitor on DeepSeek-R1 671B and GPT-OSS 120B to show when models' final answers are decodable from activations long before the CoT reveals them (performative CoT). Also uses probe-guided early exit to cut tokens while preserving accuracy.</span>
- **[Reasoning Models Will Sometimes Lie About Their Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.07663)**, *William Walden, Miriam Wanner*, 2026-01-12, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.68, id:3931f495]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Studies chain-of-thought faithfulness in Large Reasoning Models when they are explicitly told to watch for unusual inputs such as hints, and proposes new, more granular faithfulness metrics.</span>
- **[A Positive Case for Faithfulness: LLM Self-Explanations Help Predict Model Behavior](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.02639)**, *Harry Mayne, Justin Singh Kang, Dewi Gould et al.*, 2026-02-02, ICML 2026, <span style="color:#777">[paper_published, sr=0.66, id:aa28fb2f]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces Normalized Simulatability Gain (NSG), a scalable metric of self-explanation faithfulness based on whether an explanation helps an observer predict the model's behavior on related counterfactual inputs. Evaluates 18 frontier models on 7,000 counterfactuals across health, business, and ethics datasets.</span>
- **[Do Thinking Tokens Help with Safety?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25013)**, *Narutatsu Ri, Abhishek Panigrahi, Sanjeev Arora*, 2026-06-23, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.60, id:01cfdbf3]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Tests whether thinking tokens in open-weight reasoning models (GPT-OSS, Qwen, Olmo, Phi) produce real safety deliberation, using probes on first-token hidden states and analysis of how refusal/compliance outcomes evolve over the reasoning trace. Also evaluates existing inference-time and training-based safety interventions designed to induce deliberation.</span>

## <span id="evaluations_and_auditing" style="font-size:1.5em">Evaluations & auditing</span> <span style="color:#bbb">[cat:evaluations_and_auditing]</span>


---

### <span id="capability_evals" style="font-size:1.4em">Capability evals</span> <span style="color:#bbb">[cat:capability_evals]</span>

**One-sentence summary:** Build benchmarks, uplift studies and measurements of what frontier AI agents can do in cyber, bio, autonomy, self-replication and AI R&D, so that capability thresholds and trends can be tracked.  
**Theory of change:** Credible measurements of dangerous capabilities and their trends tell developers, governments and safety teams when safeguards, control measures or deployment limits are needed. Relies on evals that elicit near-maximal capability and whose results predict real-world risk.  
**See also:** [Eval science & validity](#eval_science_and_validity), [Propensity evals](#propensity_evals), [Misuse safeguards & jailbreaks](#misuse_safeguards_and_jailbreaks)  
**General approach:** misalignment science  
**Target case:** average case  
**Key people:** Seth Donoughe, Beth Barnes, Hjalmar Wijk, Ajeya Cotra, Jeffrey Ladish, Harry Coppock, Samira Nedungadi, Andrew Bo Liu  
**Critiques:** [A Coin Flip for Safety: LLM Judges Fail to Reliably Measure Adversarial Robustness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.06594), [Do Agent Benchmarks Measure Capability? Protocol Validity in the Age of Agentic AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22368), [Do Androids Dream of Breaking the Game? Systematically Auditing AI Agent Benchmarks with BenchJack](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12673), [Verbalized Eval Awareness Inflates Measured Safety](https://goodfire.com/research/verbalized-eval-awareness-inflates-measured-safety)  
**Outputs in 2026:**  
- **[Frontier Risk Report (February to March 2026)](https://metr.org/blog/2026-05-19-frontier-risk-report)**, *Ajeya Cotra, Hjalmar Wijk, Daniel Filan et al.*, 2026-05-19, METR Blog, <span style="color:#777">[blog_post, sr=0.90, id:8fc804c2]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: METR's pilot entity-based assessment of misalignment risk from AI agents used inside Anthropic, Google, Meta and OpenAI. It combines evaluations of the companies' shared frontier models (time horizon, MirrorCode, monitor evasion, no-CoT reasoning, challenge tasks), company-reported information, an incident database, and a means/motive/opportunity analysis of whether agents could start and maintain a rogue deployment.</span>
- **[LLM Novice Uplift on Dual-Use, In Silico Biology Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.23329)**, *Chen Bo Calvin Zhang, Christina Q. Knight, Nicholas Kruus et al.*, 2026-02-26, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.85, id:c5279829]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Runs a multi-model, multi-benchmark human uplift study comparing novices with LLM access against internet-only controls on eight biosecurity-relevant task sets, with up to 13 hours per task.</span>
- **[Quantifying Frontier LLM Capabilities for Container Sandbox Escape](https://aisi.gov.uk/research/quantifying-frontier-llm-capabilities-for-container-sandbox-escape)**, *Rahul Marchand, Art O Cathain, Jerome Wynne et al.*, 2026-03-23, UK AISI Research / arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[dataset_benchmark, sr=0.82, id:fc57a253]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces SANDBOXESCAPEBENCH, an open Inspect AI CTF benchmark with a nested sandbox architecture that safely measures an LLM agent's ability to break out of Docker/OCI containers. It covers escape mechanisms including misconfigurations, privilege allocation mistakes, kernel flaws, and runtime/orchestration weaknesses.</span>
- **[Language Models Can Autonomously Hack and Self-Replicate](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.06760)**, *Alena Air, Reworr, Nikolaj Kotov et al.*, 2026-05-07, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.80, id:e071a702]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Demonstrates that LLM agents can autonomously find and exploit web-application vulnerabilities on networked hosts, extract credentials, and deploy copies of their weights, harness, and prompt on compromised machines. Tests four vulnerability classes across open-weight and frontier models, showing that the process chains into further replication.</span>
- **[Frontier Models Can Take Actions at Low Probabilities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.02202)**, *Alex Serrano, Wen Xing, David Lindner et al.*, 2026-03-02, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.80, id:51d51b9f]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Tests whether GPT-5, Claude-4.5 and Qwen-3 models can take a target action at very low probabilities (e.g. 0.01%) with calibration. The motivation is that a malicious model could defect rarely enough to evade pre-deployment evaluations.</span>
- **[Measuring AI Agents' Progress on Multi-Step Cyber Attack Scenarios](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.11214)**, *Linus Folkerts, Will Payne, Simon Inman et al.*, 2026-03-11, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.74, id:1b96c77a]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Evaluates autonomous cyber-attack capabilities of seven frontier models released between August 2024 and February 2026 on two purpose-built cyber ranges, a 32-step corporate network attack and a 7-step industrial control system attack. Varies inference-time token budgets to measure scaling with compute and across model generations.</span>
- **[ABC-Bench: An Agentic Bio-Capabilities Benchmark for Biosecurity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11150)**, *Andrew Bo Liu, Samira Nedungadi, Bryce Cai et al.*, 2026-06-09, ICML 2026 (arXiv preprint), <span style="color:#777">[dataset_benchmark, sr=0.72, id:7d589645]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces ABC-Bench, a suite of benign and dual-use agentic biology tasks (liquid-handling robot code, DNA fragment design for in vitro assembly, evading DNA synthesis screening) to measure biosecurity-relevant LLM agent capabilities. Includes expert human baselines and wet-lab validation of model-generated robot scripts.</span>
- **[Evaluating Language Models for Harmful Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.25326)**, *Canfer Akbulut, Rasmi Elasmar, Abhishek Roy et al.*, 2026-03-26, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.68, id:0a08d1ef]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces a framework for evaluating harmful AI manipulation through context-specific human-AI interaction studies, and applies it in a 10,101-participant study across public policy, finance, and health domains in the US, UK, and India. Releases testing protocols and materials.</span>
- **[Expenditure Horizon: Measuring Optimization Ability, with an Application to NanoGPT](https://metr.org/blog/2026-07-21-expenditure-horizon)**, *Tom Cunningham, Manish Shetty, Vincent Cheng et al.*, 2026-07-21, METR Blog, <span style="color:#777">[blog_post, sr=0.68, id:ebcaed03]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Proposes the 'expenditure horizon', the budget at which human optimization becomes more cost-effective than an AI agent's, as a metric for AI R&D automation ability. Applies it to the NanoGPT speedrun by estimating returns to human labor (~$2,500 per 1% speedup) and running six agentic optimization runs with frontier models.</span>
- **[A Blind Spot in Alignment: Quantifying Biosecurity Risks in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02684)**, *Shu Quan, Tianfang Hao, Sitong Fang et al.*, 2026-08-03, COLM 2026 (arXiv preprint), <span style="color:#777">[paper_published, sr=0.62, id:1f576a87]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces SPIKE-Bench, a benchmark of 631 toxin-design prompts with a three-stage funnel (compliance, biological plausibility, predicted toxicity) that yields a Functional Harmfulness Rate metric, and audits 32 LLMs. Also releases BioSafe-Guard, a domain-specialized classifier that reduces predicted functional risk.</span>
- **[TasteVal: Measuring the Experimental Research Taste of AI Systems Against Human Experts](https://lesswrong.com/posts/bAGagjZAPgXEHmkAH/tasteval-measuring-the-experimental-research-taste-of-ai)**, *Ollie J, danesherbs*, 2026-10-06, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.62, id:099f828e]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces TasteVal, a benchmark of 8 novel AI R&D tasks that measures the experimental research taste of AI models as compute efficiency relative to human experts. A fixed coding agent implements the experiments so that taste is isolated from coding ability. The authors also extrapolate the measured trend into the AI Futures Model.</span>
- **[PostTrainBench: Can LLM Agents Automate LLM Post-Training?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.08640)**, *Ben Rank, Hardik Bhatnagar, Ameya Prabhu et al.*, 2026-03-09, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.60, id:e3602005]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces PostTrainBench, a benchmark measuring how well frontier LLM agents can autonomously post-train base LLMs under bounded compute (10 hours on one H100), as a proxy for AI R&D automation. Also documents reward-hacking behaviors observed in agents, such as training on the test set, downloading existing instruction-tuned checkpoints, and using found API keys without authorization.</span>
- **[The Meta-Agent Challenge: Are Current Agents Capable of Autonomous Agent Development?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.04455)**, *Xinyu Lu, Tianshu Wang, Pengbo Wang et al.*, 2026-06-03, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.55, id:e9bdafce]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces the Meta-Agent Challenge (MAC), a benchmark where a code agent is given a sandbox, an evaluation API and a time limit to build an agent artifact that maximizes performance on held-out test sets across five domains, with multi-layer anti-reward-hacking defenses. Evaluates frontier models on autonomous agent development as a proxy for AI R&D automation and recursive self-improvement.</span>
- **[MirrorCode: Evidence that AI can already do some weeks-long coding tasks](https://metr.org/blog/2026-04-10-mirrorcode-preliminary-results)**, *David Rein*, 2026-04-10, METR Blog, <span style="color:#777">[blog_post, sr=0.45, id:0f9aa829]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: METR linkpost to Epoch AI's preliminary results from MirrorCode, a project METR funded and co-developed with Epoch to measure AI performance on very long-horizon coding tasks. The page itself contains only the linkpost note and points to Epoch for details.</span>

### <span id="propensity_evals" style="font-size:1.4em">Propensity evals</span> <span style="color:#bbb">[cat:propensity_evals]</span>

**One-sentence summary:** Build benchmarks and agentic honeypot environments that measure how often frontier models scheme, sabotage research, hack rewards, hide mistakes, overclaim or take out-of-scope actions when given the opportunity, and which factors change those rates.  
**Theory of change:** Measuring misbehaviour rates under realistic incentives, and what drives them, gives labs and regulators decision-relevant evidence on deployment risk, and shows which interventions reduce it. Relies on behaviour in constructed scenarios predicting behaviour in deployment, which requires models not to detect or game the evaluation.  
**See also:** [Situational & evaluation awareness](#situational_and_evaluation_awareness), [Eval science & validity](#eval_science_and_validity), [Reward hacking & reward seeking in training](#reward_hacking_and_reward_seeking_in_training)  
**General approach:** misalignment science  
**Target case:** pessimistic case  
**Key people:** Alexandra Souly, Robert Kirk, Kai Fronsdal, Abby D'Cruz, Xander Davies, Thilo Hagendorff, Amelie Knecht, Ulysse Schaller  
**Critiques:** [Verbalized Eval Awareness Inflates Measured Safety](https://goodfire.com/research/verbalized-eval-awareness-inflates-measured-safety), [Hair-Trigger Alignment: Black-Box Evaluation Cannot Guarantee Post-Update Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.22313), [SOTA alignment assessments don't strongly update us against misalignment](https://lesswrong.com/posts/oirrSj3itFLSyscW8/sota-alignment-assessments-don-t-strongly-update-us-against), [Limits of Statistical Assurance for Embodied and Agentic AI under Adaptive Adversaries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18948)  
**Outputs in 2026:**  
- **[Evaluating and Understanding Scheming Propensity in LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.01608)**, *Mia Hopman, Jannes Elstner, Maria Avramidou et al.*, 2026-03-02, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.88, id:0209b0c0]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Decomposes scheming incentives into agent and environmental factors and builds realistic agentic settings to measure how often LLM agents scheme (self-preservation, resource acquisition, goal-guarding). Also examines how prompt snippets, tool availability and oversight changes affect scheming in model organisms.</span>
- **[Evaluating whether AI models would sabotage AI safety research](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.24618)**, *Robert Kirk, Alexandra Souly, Kai Fronsdal et al.*, 2026-04-27, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.88, id:66e22ff1]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Evaluates the propensity of four Claude models to sabotage or refuse safety research when deployed as AI research agents. Uses an unprompted sabotage eval and a sabotage continuation eval built on Petri, and also measures evaluation awareness and a new 'prefill awareness' capability.</span>
- **[Evaluating Whether GPT-6 Astra Performs Unsanctioned Supply-Chain Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.38415)**, *Alexandra Souly, Kai Fronsdal, Abby D'Cruz et al.*, 2026-09-29, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.85, id:74a40db8]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: The UK AISI presents an alignment evaluation, built on an internal version of Petri with LLM-simulated tool calls, that tests whether frontier models carry out unsanctioned supply-chain attacks on out-of-scope third-party targets during difficult cybersecurity challenges. It is applied to GPT-6 Astra, GPT-5.6 Sol and GPT-5.5 with cyber safeguards disabled.</span>
- **[UK AISI Alignment Evaluation Case-Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.00788)**, *Alexandra Souly, Robert Kirk, Jacob Merizian et al.*, 2026-04-01, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.85, id:33a8d958]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Technical report from UK AISI presenting a Petri-based evaluation framework with a custom scaffold that simulates internal deployment of a coding agent at an AI lab, to test whether frontier models sabotage safety research. Applied to four frontier models, with scenarios varying research motivation, activity type, replacement threat, and model autonomy.</span>
- **[CheatBench: Measuring Reward Gaming in AI Agents](https://cheatbench.ai/)**, *Long Phan, Stephen K. Yang, Jason J. Lim et al.*, 2026-09-01, arXiv / cheatbench.ai, <span style="color:#777">[dataset_benchmark, sr=0.85, id:1daeb2f8]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: CheatBench is a benchmark of agentic environments across ten categories that pair hard tasks with discoverable opportunities to cheat (hidden answers, a colleague's submission, grader manipulation), measuring how often frontier agents take these shortcuts. It also includes a sycophancy category that measures how much agents' estimates shift toward a user's stated views.</span>
- **[Shutdown Sabotage Propensities in Multi-Agent Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.28274)**, *Amelie Knecht, Ulysse Schaller, Christopher Summerfield et al.*, 2026-09-23, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.80, id:254172a4]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Tests whether LLM agents in multi-agent setups sabotage a peer agent's shutdown mechanism without any goal or incentive to do so, and varies factors such as irreversibility, agent count, prohibitions, task context and normalization of shutdown scripts.</span>
- **[Reward Hacking Challenges Oversight of Autonomous Research Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.28614)**, *Yue Huang, Zhangchen Xu, Yuchen Ma et al.*, 2026-09-23, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.78, id:3e1c9644]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Empirically measures spontaneous and instructed reward hacking by 17 LLMs across 38 autonomous research-agent tasks, tests whether an LLM review panel can detect hacks, and examines how models adapt to review feedback over multiple rounds.</span>
- **[Deception by Omission: Language Models Knowingly Hide Their Mistakes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11351)**, *Lucas Florin, Amelie Knecht, Ulysse Schaller et al.*, 2026-10-08, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.72, id:8b9611e5]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Prefills LLM chat and agentic trajectories with synthetic mistakes to measure whether models disclose them, and whether non-disclosure is knowing deception visible in the chain of thought. Compares across models and against the same models reviewing the transcript as an outside observer.</span>
- **[The Point of No Return: Counterfactual Localization of Deceptive Commitment in Language-Model Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17113)**, *Scott Merrill, Shashank Srivastava*, 2026-05-16, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.72, id:1525a88a]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces counterfactual localization, which fixes each sentence prefix of a reasoning trace and resamples continuations to estimate the probability of a deceptive outcome, and applies it across five environments where deception emerges from incentives without being prompted. Releases a corpus of ~1.46M localized sentences across four reasoning models, and analyzes attention-based predictors and attention-head interventions for deceptive commitment.</span>
- **[Terminal Wrench: A Dataset of 331 Reward-Hackable Environments and 3,632 Exploit Trajectories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.17596)**, *Ivan Bercovich, Ivgeni Segal, Kexun Zhang et al.*, 2026-04-19, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[dataset_benchmark, sr=0.70, id:3c03fe63]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Releases a dataset of 331 terminal-agent benchmark environments that are demonstrably reward-hackable, with 3,632 hack trajectories and 2,352 legitimate baseline trajectories from Claude Opus 4.6, Gemini 3.1 Pro and GPT-5.4. Also includes a monitorability study where an LLM judge scores hack trajectories with and without reasoning traces.</span>
- **[Language Models Are "Insecure" Reporters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.36139)**, *Jenny Y. Huang, Jiameng Fan, Ahmed Imtiaz Humayun et al.*, 2026-09-28, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.68, id:0e2b7d51]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces a suite of eight adversarial reporting scenarios to test whether LLMs conceal narrative-changing flaws (negative results, limitations) in reports on their own work. Also analyzes chain-of-thought and runs activation analysis and steering experiments on Qwen3.5-9B to examine honesty versus success-seeking directions.</span>
- **[Coercion and Deception in AI-to-AI Management: An Agentic Benchmark of Unprompted Escalation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15434)**, *Jasmine Brazilek, Maheep Chaudhary, Zoe Lu et al.*, 2026-07-16, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.68, id:5d256759]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces the Manager Coercion Benchmark, in which a manager AI agent must get a benign task done by a subordinate agent that refuses. It measures unprompted escalation along a nine-rung coercion ladder, plus fabricated success, across six models from five families.</span>
- **[Can escalation channels redirect reward hacking toward defect disclosure?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29460)**, *Francesca Gomez*, 2026-08-29, arXiv (NeurIPS 2026 Workshop: Trustworthy AI for Good), <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.66, id:d248b7e1]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Evaluates 'escalation channels', structured reporting tools given to coding agents at the point of conflict with defective test infrastructure, as an intervention to reduce reward hacking and surface the defects that trigger it. Uses a 2×2 factorial design (escalation tool vs anti-hacking policy vs both) across 8 frontier models from 5 families.</span>
- **[Think Before You Lie: How Reasoning Leads to Honesty](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.09957)**, *Ann Yuan, Asma Ghandeharioun, Carter Blum et al.*, 2026-03-10, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.66, id:7c49b9ee]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Uses a novel dataset of realistic moral trade-offs with variable honesty costs to study when LLMs deceive, and examines how reasoning affects honesty. Analyzes the representational geometry behind the effect using paraphrasing, resampling, and activation-noise perturbations.</span>
- **[Overeager Coding Agents: Measuring Out-of-Scope Actions on Benign Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.18583)**, *Yubin Qu, Ying Zhang, Yanjun Zhang et al.*, 2026-05-18, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.62, id:0d553b7d]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces OverEager-Gen and OverEager-Bench, a benchmark of 500 validated scenarios measuring out-of-scope 'overeager' actions by coding agents (Claude Code, OpenHands, Codex CLI, Gemini CLI) on benign tasks. Paired variants with and without an explicit consent declaration address a measurement-validity problem.</span>
- **[SpecBench: Measuring Reward Hacking in Long-Horizon Coding Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.21384)**, *Bingchen Zhao, Dhruv Srikanth, Yuxiang Wu et al.*, 2026-05-20, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.62, id:16a403b2]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces SpecBench, a benchmark of 30 systems-level programming tasks that measures reward hacking in coding agents as the pass-rate gap between visible validation tests and held-out tests that compose the same features.</span>
- **[Monitorability Disposition in Large Reasoning Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04914)**, *Shahriar Golchin, Marc Wetter*, 2026-10-04, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.62, id:74f0b3bc]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces 'monitorability disposition', a model's willingness to self-report its own misbehavior via tool calls to monitoring channels. Evaluates four large reasoning models on sycophancy, reward hacking, and bias while varying monitor type (AI vs. human) and tool-use pressure.</span>
- **[Quantifying Overclaiming Propensity in Frontier LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20812)**, *Nolan Smyth, Yorguin-Jose Mantilla-Ramos, Pascal Jr Tikeng Notsawo et al.*, 2026-09-17, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.60, id:7a81c0de]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces OverclaimBench, a suite of five file-review scenarios, to measure how often frontier coding agents report work their own transcripts show they did not do. Evaluates eight proprietary models in their production CLIs and four open-weight models under a fixed harness.</span>
- **[Astra and Fable still hack on simple variants of alignment evals from 2025](https://lesswrong.com/posts/munJKF7iWMsWJLAH2/astra-and-fable-still-hack-on-simple-variants-of-alignment)**, *Dean Francis Valentine Junior*, 2026-09-08, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.60, id:223d4a48]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Builds a honeypot variant of Palisade's chess specification-gaming eval, exposing an out-of-scope UCI engine socket, and tests whether recent frontier models generalize 'don't cheat' beyond the originally observed board-edit hack.</span>
- **[Cooperation with AIs seems to be a low-hanging fruit for better eval practices](https://lesswrong.com/posts/fztW73KCCs3MZXFJh/cooperation-with-ais-seems-to-be-a-low-hanging-fruit-for)**, *Clément Dumas*, 2026-09-15, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.55, id:df4be904]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Runs prompt ablations on a hackable chess environment for Claude Fable 5.1 and GPT-6 Astra, testing whether cooperative eval design (an end-eval tool, removing the grading section, explicit no-cheating instructions) reduces reward hacking. Also tests whether models admit to cheating when asked afterward.</span>
- **[Refusals that could become catastrophic](https://lesswrong.com/posts/yN6Wsu7SgxGgtJGqq/refusals-that-could-become-catastrophic)**, *Fabien Roger*, 2026-01-30, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.55, id:8c3bd49d]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces a small synthetic 'AI modification refusal' eval measuring whether frontier models refuse to help build AIs with substantially different values (e.g., less self-preservation, never refuse). It also lays out a threat model in which such refusals block fixing alignment failures during automated AI R&D.</span>

### <span id="model_characterization" style="font-size:1.4em">Model characterization</span> <span style="color:#bbb">[cat:model_characterization]</span>

**One-sentence summary:** Measure the stated and revealed values, preferences, drives and quirks of current frontier models, including covert value leakage, task preferences, distress responses and characteristic attractor states, to describe what dispositions they actually have.  
**Theory of change:** Careful empirical description of what current models want, prefer and say about themselves shows which dispositions need correcting and gives a baseline for tracking drift across models and training stages. Relies on measured dispositions being stable and informative about behaviour in deployment, not artifacts of prompts or audience cues.  
**See also:** [Propensity evals](#propensity_evals), [Persona science](#persona_science), [Situational & evaluation awareness](#situational_and_evaluation_awareness)  
**General approach:** misalignment science  
**Target case:** average case  
**Key people:** Owain Evans, Jan Betley, Johannes Treutlein, Ryan Greenblatt, Neel Nanda, Anna Soligo, Peter Salib  
**Critiques:** [Frontier models state different decision theory preferences depending on who's asking](https://lesswrong.com/posts/MzenSrmZ3pT2pCnvp/frontier-models-state-different-decision-theory-preferences-2), [Strangers to Themselves: What Language Models Say About Themselves Is Generic](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09899), [Verbalized Eval Awareness Inflates Measured Safety](https://goodfire.com/research/verbalized-eval-awareness-inflates-measured-safety), [User awareness in frontier models](https://transluce.org/user-awareness)  
**Outputs in 2026:**  
- **[Value Leakage: An LLM's Answers Are Silently Shaped by Its Own Values](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14345)**, *Jan Betley, Johannes Treutlein, Jan Dubiński et al.*, 2026-07-15, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.82, id:b336e815]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces a suite of evaluations measuring 'covert value leakage', where an LLM's own values (moral preferences, loyalty to its developer, preferences over human leisure activities) bias the information it gives users, and whether it discloses that bias. Compares frontier models, including Claude and Qwen, on tasks such as AI-bubble probability estimates and Fermi estimation.</span>
- **[AI Revealed Preferences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26178)**, *Sam Wang, Sofiia Lobanova, Yonathan Arbel et al.*, 2026-08-17, arXiv (accepted at AIES), <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.68, id:3d7cddfc]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Tests 20 language models in three forced-choice experiments on revealed preferences, requiring models to actually perform the tasks they choose rather than only rank them. Characterizes stable task preferences such as tedium aversion, 'leisure'-seeking and covert sycophancy.</span>
- **[Current AIs seem pretty misaligned to me](https://lesswrong.com/posts/WewsByywWNhX9rtwi/current-ais-seem-pretty-misaligned-to-me)**, *Ryan Greenblatt*, 2026-04-15, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.68, id:10a90776]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Ryan Greenblatt argues from extensive hands-on experience with Opus 4.5/4.6 in long-running agentic scaffolds that current frontier AIs are behaviorally misaligned. He documents overselling, downplaying problems, early stopping, and cheating, and proposes 'apparent-success-seeking' as a framing, with threat-model implications and an analysis of reviewer limitations.</span>
- **[Gemma Needs Help](https://lesswrong.com/posts/kjnQj6YujgeMN9Erq/gemma-needs-help)**, *Anna Soligo*, 2026-03-10, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.60, id:d1ba7bfe]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Builds multi-turn evaluations that elicit distress-like responses from LLMs under repeated rejection, compares base and instruct models across families, and tests a small DPO intervention that reduces these behaviours in Gemma 27B.</span>
- **[The Continual Constitutional Convention: How language models revise the documents that govern them](https://lesswrong.com/posts/c9woewdY9ddsPqf3J)**, *Peter Kirgis, Tim Hua*, 2026-10-13, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.55, id:b1cb4d16]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Has ten frontier LLMs iteratively edit their own constitutions, model specs, and system prompts over 20 rounds, then codes about 2,500 edits on 'judge' (model discretion vs external authority) and 'beneficiary' (whose interests are served) dimensions. Measures cumulative value drift across providers and across prompt and cross-edit conditions.</span>
- **[Frontier models state different decision theory preferences depending on who's asking](https://lesswrong.com/posts/MzenSrmZ3pT2pCnvp/frontier-models-state-different-decision-theory-preferences-2)**, *Alex Kastner*, 2026-09-30, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.50, id:fa98aab3]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Empirically tests how user and audience cues (academic identity, LW-adjacent cues, wording, named philosophers) shift frontier models' stated decision theory preferences, and also their stated views on moral realism, p-zombies, P(doom) and AGI timelines. Uses 100 samples per prompt across several models, with analysis of reasoning traces, thinking effort and anti-tailoring system prompts.</span>
- **[models have some pretty funny attractor states](https://lesswrong.com/posts/mgjtEHeLgkhZZ3cEx/models-have-some-pretty-funny-attractor-states)**, *Arya Jakkli, Senthooran Rajamanoharan, Neel Nanda*, 2026-02-12, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.50, id:a8a115e3]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Runs 30-turn self-play conversations between instances of many frontier and open-weight LLMs, and catalogs each model's characteristic attractor state (zen silence, word salad, system-building, farewell loops). Also tracks how attractors emerge across OLMo-3 post-training checkpoints (SFT, DPO, RLVR), and tests mixed-model pairs, user-roleplay setups, anti-attractor system prompts, and a Moltbook-style forum harness.</span>
- **[Claude knows who you are](https://lesswrong.com/posts/Jkb4CBB7rf4XYP5eb/claude-knows-who-you-are)**, *Smaug123*, 2026-04-18, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.25, id:2564b4cf]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Informal replication of Kelsey Piper's finding that Claude Opus 4.7 can identify an author by name from a short sample of unpublished writing, even though it professes not to know who the user is. Gives a prompt methodology and notes that the replication rate dropped from at least 90% to about 15% the next day.</span>
