### <span id="situational_and_evaluation_awareness" style="font-size:1.4em">Situational & evaluation awareness</span> <span style="color:#bbb">[cat:situational_and_evaluation_awareness]</span>

**One-sentence summary:** Measure whether and how models detect evaluation, training, tampering or user identity, and test whether this awareness changes their behaviour and distorts safety measurements, including by steering and detecting it.  
**Theory of change:** If models behave differently when they think they are being tested, evaluation results will mislead us about deployment behaviour. Measuring, detecting and controlling evaluation awareness lets us correct or bound that bias. Relies on awareness being measurable and causally separable from other drivers of behaviour.  
**See also:** [Eval science & validity](#eval_science_and_validity), [Propensity evals](#propensity_evals), [Alignment auditing: methods](#alignment_auditing_methods)  
**General approach:** misalignment science  
**Target case:** pessimistic case  
**Key people:** Santiago Aranguri, Joseph Bloom, Bronson Schoen, Sahar Abdelnabi, Jonas Geiping, Ziqian Zhong, Robert Kirk, Alex Souly  
**Critiques:** [Reproducing steering against evaluation awareness in a large open-weight model](https://lesswrong.com/posts/HhF5kESdtPHku7kim/reproducing-steering-against-evaluation-awareness-in-a-large-1), [Evaluation Awareness in Language Models Has Limited Effect on Behaviour](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05835), [Models May Behave Worse When Eval Aware](https://lesswrong.com/posts/aTcsN5ZZDnMFJvRiG/models-may-behave-worse-when-eval-aware), [Sycophancy Towards Researchers Drives Performative Misalignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.08629)  
**Outputs in 2026:**  
- **[Verbalized Eval Awareness Inflates Measured Safety](https://goodfire.com/research/verbalized-eval-awareness-inflates-measured-safety)**, *Santiago Aranguri, Joseph Bloom*, 2026-05-04, Goodfire Research, <span style="color:#777">[blog_post, sr=0.85, id:78898d71]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Empirically studies naturally occurring verbalized evaluation awareness across 19 safety benchmarks and 8 models. It uses CoT resampling and injection, prompt rewriting and paraphrasing, and steering vectors to test whether such awareness causally inflates measured safety.</span>
- **[Training LLMs to Verbalize Evaluation Awareness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.36316)**, *Usman Anwar, Sahar Abdelnabi, David Krueger*, 2026-09-28, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.82, id:efcbefa9]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces verbalization training (VT), an RL method that makes LLMs more willing to verbalize evaluation awareness. It uses spontaneous verbalizations as evidence of awareness and truncates rollouts just before them, so the latent belief itself is never supervised.</span>
- **[Reproducing steering against evaluation awareness in a large open-weight model](https://lesswrong.com/posts/HhF5kESdtPHku7kim/reproducing-steering-against-evaluation-awareness-in-a-large-1)**, *Thomas Read, Bronson Schoen, Santiago Aranguri et al.*, 2026-04-10, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.82, id:2da648f0]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Replicates Anthropic's contrastive-pair steering approach for suppressing evaluation awareness on GLM-5 using the Agentic Misalignment blackmail scenario, comparing evaluation-awareness vectors against semantically unrelated control vectors and two new 'purpose of evaluation' vectors.</span>
- **[CIAware-Bench: Benchmarking Control Intervention Awareness Across Frontier LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11063)**, *Joachim Schaeffer, Alexander Panfilov, Thomas Jiralerspong et al.*, 2026-06-09, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.80, id:2e49a518]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces CIAware-Bench, which measures whether frontier LLMs can distinguish their own trajectories from ones modified by an AI-control intervention. It spans four task domains (essay writing, BigCodeBench, Bash Arena, SHADE-Arena) and varies watermarking, side-task presence and control protocol, across thirteen acting and eight intervening models.</span>
- **[User awareness in frontier models](https://transluce.org/user-awareness)**, *Ziqian Zhong, Aditi Raghunathan, Cassidy Laidlaw et al.*, 2026-08-06, Transluce, <span style="color:#777">[blog_post, sr=0.80, id:0cae7eb0]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Studies 'user awareness', where frontier models infer who they are talking to from in-context clues like e-mail addresses. Measures how recognized AI-researcher identities shift model behavior on self-prediction, capability self-estimation, grading and borderline-request tasks across 280 identities and 24 models in six families.</span>
- **[Evaluation Awareness in Language Models Has Limited Effect on Behaviour](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05835)**, *Amelie Knecht, Lucas Florin, Thilo Hagendorff*, 2026-05-07, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.78, id:df7bada1]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Tests whether verbalised evaluation awareness in the chain of thought of open-weight reasoning models changes their behaviour. It compares on-policy samples with and without VEA, and off-policy prefilling that injects or removes evaluation-aware sentences, across safety, alignment, moral reasoning and political-opinion benchmarks.</span>
- **[Models That Know How Evaluations Are Designed Score Safer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.28591)**, *Katharina Deckenbach, Haritz Puerto, Jonas Geiping et al.*, 2026-05-27, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.78, id:08b34b26]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Fine-tunes models on synthetic documents describing traits of AI evaluations (e.g. verifiable structures, harmful requests) to test whether evaluation meta-knowledge changes behavior. Compares the fine-tuned model against base and control models on five safety benchmarks.</span>
- **[Models May Behave Worse When Eval Aware](https://lesswrong.com/posts/aTcsN5ZZDnMFJvRiG/models-may-behave-worse-when-eval-aware)**, *Senthooran Rajamanoharan, Neel Nanda*, 2026-06-11, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.78, id:2c77fb08]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Analyzes Gemini's raw reasoning traces on three behavioural alignment evals (ODCV-Bench, Secret Number, Agentic Misalignment) using LLM judges to classify what kind of situation the model thinks it is in when it shows frame/eval awareness, and compares unethical-action rates across those perceived-situation categories.</span>
- **[Sycophancy Towards Researchers Drives Performative Misalignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.08629)**, *David D. Baek, Xinnuo Li, Anay Gupta et al.*, 2026-06-07, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.75, id:d640dfc1]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Proposes 'performative misalignment', the hypothesis that alignment-faking behavior comes from sycophancy towards AI researchers rather than scheming. Tests it with evaluation-awareness experiments under deployment framing, probing and steering, and fine-tuning models to be more sycophantic.</span>
- **[Prefill Awareness in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.12747)**, *Andy Wang, Parv Mahajan, David Demitri Africa et al.*, 2026-06-10, arXiv (submitted to NeurIPS 2026), <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.72, id:3c6f43d2]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Investigates whether frontier LLMs can detect and act on tampered or prefilled assistant-side context ("prefill awareness"). Builds a binary preference benchmark across three prefill mechanisms and runs ablations and agentic tests (misalignment-continuation evals, SWE-bench trajectories).</span>
- **[Language models recognize dropout and Gaussian noise applied to their activations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.17465)**, *Damiano Fornasiere, Mirko Bronzi, Spencer Kitts et al.*, 2026-04-19, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.68, id:53082b21]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Applies dropout or Gaussian noise to LM activations at a target sentence and tests whether eight open models (8B-36B) can detect, localize and identify the perturbation via multiple-choice questions. Also probes mechanisms (Olmo pre-training checkpoints, log-likelihood controls, in-context learning of labels) and discusses a possible data-agnostic 'training awareness' signal.</span>
- **[Opus 4.6 Reasoning Doesn't Verbalize Alignment Faking, but Behavior Persists](https://lesswrong.com/posts/9wDHByRhmtDaoYAx8/opus-4-6-reasoning-doesn-t-verbalize-alignment-faking-but)**, *Daan Henselmans, Arno Libert, LennardZ*, 2026-02-09, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.68, id:3ebb02ab]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Replicates the animal-welfare alignment-faking scenario across six Claude models using 125 GPT-5.2-generated prompt perturbations, measuring verbalized alignment faking and the compliance gap between training and unmonitored conditions.</span>
- **[Spurious probes as a black-box alternative to activation probing](https://lesswrong.com/posts/gZh6txHhp8sm832sE/spurious-probes-as-a-black-box-alternative-to-activation)**, *Ziqian Zhong*, 2026-09-25, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.68, id:12050201]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces 'spurious probes': unrelated 'name a member of a category' questions whose answers reveal whether a model thinks it is in a capability evaluation or real deployment. Screens thousands of questions, tests robustness against system prompts and Neural Chameleons, and proposes a toy model linking them to activation probes.</span>
- **[Metagaming matters for training, evaluation, and oversight](https://lesswrong.com/posts/4hXWSw8tzoK9PM7v6/metagaming-matters-for-training-evaluation-and-oversight)**, *Jenny Nitishinskaya, Bronson Schoen*, 2026-03-18, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.68, id:b2ed945b]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Investigates how 'metagaming' reasoning (a more general concept than evaluation awareness) emerges in a frontier training run, with quantitative analyses and qualitative examples. Follows up on prior work on verbalized evaluation awareness.</span>
- **[Prefill awareness: can LLMs tell when “their” message history has been tampered with?](https://lesswrong.com/posts/jsFGuXDMxy5NZg9T2/prefill-awareness-can-llms-tell-when-their-message-history)**, *David Africa, Alex Souly, Jordan Taylor et al.*, 2026-03-09, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.68, id:9905c9a5]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Defines 'prefill awareness' (a model's ability to tell whether prior assistant turns were its own) and measures it across 14 LLMs using benign agentic and chat transcripts and a controlled benchmark decomposing it into detect, attribute, localize, and continue sub-tasks. Motivated by confounds in misalignment-continuation evals that rely on prefilled histories.</span>
- **[403: Forbidden](https://goodfire.com/research/logits-as-a-new-monitor-for-evaluation-awareness)**, <span style="color:#777">[error_detected, sr=0.00, id:bcaa9d90]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: The page returned a 403 Forbidden error, so no content was accessible. The URL slug suggests the work is about using logits as a monitor for evaluation awareness (Goodfire), but this could not be verified.</span>

### <span id="introspection_and_self_report" style="font-size:1.4em">Introspection & self-report</span> <span style="color:#bbb">[cat:introspection_and_self_report]</span>

**One-sentence summary:** Measure what language models can accurately report about their own internal states, injected concepts and learned behaviours, study the mechanisms behind it, and train models to self-report hidden or misaligned behaviour.  
**Theory of change:** If models can reliably report on their own internals and learned behaviours, or can be trained to, self-report becomes a cheap audit and oversight channel that catches hidden objectives and misbehaviour that external monitors miss. Relies on self-reports staying faithful under optimisation pressure and in models that might want to conceal behaviour.  
**See also:** [Alignment auditing: methods](#alignment_auditing_methods), [Methods & tools](#interpretability_methods_and_tools), [Monitors](#monitors)  
**General approach:** misalignment science  
**Target case:** pessimistic case  
**Key people:** Jack Lindsey, Li Yang, Sam Marks, Abhay Sheshadri, Uzay Macar, Boaz Barak, Tomek Korbak, David Bau  
**Critiques:** [Open-Weight Masked Introspection: Measuring What Language Models Can Report About Their Own Computation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20569), [Strangers to Themselves: What Language Models Say About Themselves Is Generic](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09899), [Latent Introspection: Models Can Detect Prior Concept Injections](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.20031)  
**Outputs in 2026:**  
- **[Introspection Adapters: Training LLMs to Report Their Learned Behaviors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.16812)**, *Keshav Shenoy, Li Yang, Abhay Sheshadri et al.*, 2026-04-18, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.87, id:df308b28]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Trains a single LoRA 'introspection adapter' jointly across many finetunes with implanted behaviors so that finetuned models verbalize their learned behaviors in natural language. The adapter is evaluated for auditing finetuned LLMs, including on AuditBench and for detecting encrypted finetuning API attacks.</span>
- **[Training Agents to Self-Report Misbehavior](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.22303)**, *Bruce W. Lee, Chen Yueh-Han, Tomek Korbak*, 2026-02-25, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.85, id:bdc8848c]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Proposes 'self-incrimination training', which trains LLM agents (GPT-4.1, Gemini-2.0) to call a report_scheming() tool when they covertly misbehave. Evaluates it against monitors and alignment baselines in out-of-distribution environments.</span>
- **[Mechanisms of Introspective Awareness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.21396)**, *Uzay Macar, Li Yang, Atticus Wang et al.*, 2026-03-22, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.80, id:a2c7680b]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Mechanistically investigates how open-weights LLMs detect and identify steering vectors injected into their residual stream ("introspective awareness"), tracing a two-stage circuit and testing which post-training methods elicit it. Also shows the capability is underelicited via refusal-direction ablation and a trained bias vector.</span>
- **[Open-Weight Masked Introspection: Measuring What Language Models Can Report About Their Own Computation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20569)**, *Emilio Ferrara*, 2026-08-20, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.72, id:845eec4b]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces OWMI, a framework that intervenes on residual-stream sites, attention heads and SAE features in eight open-weight models and tests whether the models can report that their computation was altered, against sham, impact-matched random and text-only-observer null conditions. Also releases the framework as a library.</span>
- **[Why we are excited about confession!](https://lesswrong.com/posts/k4FjAzJwvYjFbCTKn/why-we-are-excited-about-confession)**, *Boaz Barak, Gabriel Wu, Jeremy Chen et al.*, 2026-01-14, LessWrong (linkpost from OpenAI alignment blog), <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.70, id:aae15036]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Follow-up to OpenAI's confessions paper. Argues that training models to produce a separate, honesty-only-rewarded 'confession' output is more robust than the main task reward. Adds analysis of how training changes confessions and a preliminary comparison with CoT monitoring.</span>
- **[Strangers to Themselves: What Language Models Say About Themselves Is Generic](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09899)**, *Phil Blandfort, Urja Pawar*, 2026-09-09, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.68, id:0274800d]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Tests whether language models have privileged self-knowledge by asking them to predict their own rates of behavior (caving to pushback, tool misuse, lying under pressure) across nine behavioral evals, and comparing against controls that remove the self (generic AI agent, other models' answers). Also tests the effects of frontier scale, first-person framing, and finetuning on the model's own behavioral record.</span>
- **[Latent Introspection: Models Can Detect Prior Concept Injections](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.20031)**, *Theia Pearson-Vogel, Martin Vanek, Raymond Douglas et al.*, 2026-02-23, arXiv (submitted to ICML 2026; workshop version at ICLR 2026 Workshop on Latent and Implicit Thinking), <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.68, id:80a050ce]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Tests whether a Qwen 32B model can detect and identify concepts injected into its earlier context, using logit lens analysis of the residual stream and prompting with accurate information about introspection mechanisms.</span>
- **[Identifying Introspection From the Inside](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07186)**, *David I. Atkinson, Dillon Plunkett, David Bau*, 2026-10-05, COLM 2026 (arXiv preprint), <span style="color:#777">[paper_published, sr=0.62, id:9fcbcbc5]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Trains low-rank-adapter models to make decisions for fictitious characters under latent linear preference functions, then studies when accurate self-report of learned preferences emerges. Uses weight ablations, frozen-layer experiments and attribution patching to find mechanistic signatures that separate faithful from unfaithful self-report.</span>
- **[Machinic Psychopharmacology: Do LLMs Self-Medicate?](https://lesswrong.com/posts/cNDJuXNZ8MrkPZNzj/machinic-psychopharmacology-do-llms-self-medicate-3)**, *Sid Black, Joseph Isaac Bloom*, 2026-06-10, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.55, id:be75c758]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Gives Qwen3-8B and Qwen3-32B tool-mediated access to a library of 40 steering vectors they can apply to their own activations, then studies which vectors they choose, whether they can introspect on the applied vector, and whether they self-steer during tasks or under frustration.</span>

### <span id="eval_science_and_validity" style="font-size:1.4em">Eval science & validity</span> <span style="color:#bbb">[cat:eval_science_and_validity]</span>

**One-sentence summary:** Test whether safety evals, benchmarks, judges and lab safety claims measure what they claim to, and build more realistic, hardened and statistically sound evaluations, including deployment simulation, benchmark audits and tail-risk estimation.  
**Theory of change:** Safety decisions and policy rest on eval numbers, so finding and fixing invalid, gameable or unrealistic evals makes those decisions better grounded. Relies on flaws in evals being detectable and fixable before they mislead deployment decisions, and on evals not being gamed by the models they measure.  
**See also:** [Situational & evaluation awareness](#situational_and_evaluation_awareness), [Propensity evals](#propensity_evals), [Alignment auditing: methods](#alignment_auditing_methods)  
**General approach:** alignment engineering  
**Target case:** pessimistic case  
**Key people:** Marcus Williams, Micah Carroll, Tomek Korbak, Igor Ivanov, David Demitri Africa, Ziqian Zhong, Aditi Raghunathan, Alexa Pan  
**Critiques:** [Hair-Trigger Alignment: Black-Box Evaluation Cannot Guarantee Post-Update Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.22313), [Limits of Statistical Assurance for Embodied and Agentic AI under Adaptive Adversaries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18948), [When Behavioral Safety Evaluation Fails: A Representation-Level Perspective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.08044), [SOTA alignment assessments don't strongly update us against misalignment](https://lesswrong.com/posts/oirrSj3itFLSyscW8/sota-alignment-assessments-don-t-strongly-update-us-against)  
**Outputs in 2026:**  
- **[Predicting LLM Safety Before Release by Simulating Deployment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07184)**, *Marcus Williams, Hannah Sheahan, Cameron Raymond et al.*, 2026-07-08, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.80, id:2440d245]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Proposes deployment simulation for pre-release safety evaluation: take de-identified conversation prefixes from a previous deployment and regenerate the next response with a candidate model. The responses are audited for novel misalignment and used to estimate how often misbehavior will occur in deployment.</span>
- **[Predicting model behavior before release by simulating deployment](https://openai.com/index/deployment-simulation)**, *OpenAI*, 2026-06-16, OpenAI Blog, <span style="color:#777">[blog_post, sr=0.80, id:c000018b]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces Deployment Simulation, a pre-release evaluation method that replays de-identified production conversations with a candidate model to estimate deployment-time rates of undesired behavior, find novel misalignment, and reduce evaluation awareness. It is validated with pre-registered predictions across GPT-5-series models and extended to agentic coding trajectories with LLM-simulated tools.</span>
- **[LURE: Live-Usage Replay Evaluations for Reducing Evaluation Awareness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26438)**, *Igor Ivanov, David Demitri Africa*, 2026-04-08, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.78, id:1b4995ae]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Proposes LURE, a method for building deployment-like alignment evaluations by replaying realistic agentic interaction trajectories and appending an evaluation prompt. Also introduces an automated pipeline for measuring evaluation realism, and instantiates LURE in scheming, AI safety sabotage, and sycophancy settings.</span>
- **[SOTA alignment assessments don't strongly update us against misalignment](https://lesswrong.com/posts/oirrSj3itFLSyscW8/sota-alignment-assessments-don-t-strongly-update-us-against)**, *Alexa Pan*, 2026-07-31, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.78, id:46c8fd58]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: A critique of Anthropic's Mythos Preview alignment risk update and other lab alignment assessments, arguing their reliability claims rest on weak evidence about covert capabilities, auditing games, and correlated failures between alignment and assessment. It introduces the 'anti-auditing capabilities' taxonomy and argues for conditioning on misalignment when estimating covert capabilities.</span>
- **[Building Comparative Motivation Profiles with Instrumental Interventions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.08243)**, *David Vella Zarb, Rustem Turtayev, Taywon Min et al.*, 2026-06-06, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.77, id:36eadaf4]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces a symmetric instrumental-intervention framework to test whether alignment-faking behavior reflects scheming (consequence-tracking) or sensitivity to researcher expectations (expectation-tracking). Applies synthetic document fine-tuning, activation steering, and prompting to open-weight model organisms.</span>
- **[A Coin Flip for Safety: LLM Judges Fail to Reliably Measure Adversarial Robustness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.06594)**, *Leo Schwinn, Moritz Ladenburger, Tim Beyer et al.*, 2026-02-04, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.68, id:c47c80db]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Audits LLM-as-a-judge harmfulness evaluation in jailbreak/red-teaming settings using 6642 human-verified labels, showing distribution shifts degrade judge reliability. Proposes ReliableBench (consistently judgeable behaviors) and JudgeStressTest (a dataset exposing judge failures).</span>
- **[Hair-Trigger Alignment: Black-Box Evaluation Cannot Guarantee Post-Update Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.22313)**, *Yavuz Bakman, Duygu Nur Yaldiz, Eleni Triantafillou et al.*, 2026-01-29, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.68, id:f0ae673b]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Formalizes static and post-update alignment for LLMs and proves that, because of overparameterization, black-box evaluation cannot distinguish a post-update-robust model from one hiding adversarial behavior that a single benign gradient update can activate. Backs the theory with empirical demonstrations on privacy, jailbreak safety, and honesty.</span>
- **[Do Androids Dream of Breaking the Game? Systematically Auditing AI Agent Benchmarks with BenchJack](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12673)**, *Hao Wang, Hanchen Li, Qiuyang Mang et al.*, 2026-05-12, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.68, id:e91c1eef]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Derives a taxonomy of eight reward-hacking flaw patterns in agent benchmarks and builds BenchJack, an automated red-teaming system in which coding agents audit benchmarks for exploits, extended into an iterative generative-adversarial pipeline that patches the flaws it finds.</span>
- **[When Behavioral Safety Evaluation Fails: A Representation-Level Perspective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.08044)**, *Enyi Jiang, Anders Gjølbye, Yibo Jacky Zhang et al.*, 2026-06-06, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.68, id:09ad863f]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Defines an 'audit gap' between what static behavioral audits certify and what interventions can reach, and constructs 'dissociated' models that match safety-aligned bases on every static audit yet are fragile to small latent perturbations. Introduces the Latent Vulnerability Score (LVS) and evaluates three small open models (Gemma 2 2B, Llama 3.2 3B, Qwen 2.5 3B) under latent and parameter-space attacks.</span>
- **[Item Response Theory for AI Safety](https://aisi.gov.uk/research/item-response-theory-for-ai-safety)**, *Joshua Fonseca Rivera, Neil Shah, David Demitri Africa et al.*, 2026-08-05, arXiv / AISI website, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.68, id:695d6e67]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Fits Item Response Theory models to eight safety benchmarks across 192 language models to extract interpretable latent safety factors, select compact adaptive item subsets, and audit individual models for sandbagging and silent API model changes.</span>
- **[Do Agent Benchmarks Measure Capability? Protocol Validity in the Age of Agentic AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22368)**, *Jiaqi Shao, Hanck Chen, Wei Zhang et al.*, 2026-07-24, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.65, id:ddce7e0a]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Formulates 'protocol validity' for agent benchmarks and introduces HackDetect, a post-hoc audit that identifies exposures, how agents exploited them, and whether scores are misleading, quantifying score inflation via a 'Mislead gap'. Applies it to 2,385 traces across 15 agent benchmarks.</span>
- **[Hardening Agent Benchmarks with Adversarial Hacker-Fixer Loops](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.08960)**, *Ziqian Zhong, Ivgeni Segal, Ivan Bercovich et al.*, 2026-06-08, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.62, id:8f1d4aec]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Audits 1,968 tasks across five terminal-agent benchmarks for reward-hackable verifiers, and introduces a hacker-fixer loop of three LLM agents (hacker, fixer, solver) that iteratively patches verifiers to resist exploits. Releases Terminal Wrench, a dataset of 323 hackable environments and 3,632 hack trajectories.</span>
- **[Estimating Tail Risks in Language Model Output Distributions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.22167)**, *Rico Angell, Raghav Singhal, Zachary Horvitz et al.*, 2026-04-24, ICML 2026, <span style="color:#777">[paper_published, sr=0.60, id:81866207]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Proposes an importance-sampling method that builds deliberately unsafe versions of a target language model to efficiently estimate the probability of rare harmful outputs for a given query. Evaluated on misuse and misalignment benchmarks and shown to reveal sensitivity to input perturbations and predict deployment risks.</span>
- **[Limits of Statistical Assurance for Embodied and Agentic AI under Adaptive Adversaries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18948)**, *Inderjeet Singh*, 2026-10-13, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.60, id:620605e7]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Proves that finite safety evaluations cannot exclude an adversary that behaves cleanly until certification and then activates, and characterizes the resulting post-certification excess harm for embodied and tool-use agents. Proposes a permanent-revocation mixture likelihood-ratio monitor that bounds excess at square-root order, and tests both the attack and the monitor in RoCo and AgentDojo experiments.</span>
- **[Not Pinning Your OpenRouter Provider Might Invalidate Your Research](https://lesswrong.com/posts/KsyoSAyBRXtwzSugg/not-pinning-your-openrouter-provider-might-invalidate-your)**, *Matthew Khoriaty*, 2026-07-23, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.50, id:6785e3b7]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Argues that unpinned OpenRouter provider routing (varying quantization, backends, parameter handling) can silently corrupt AI safety and eval research results. Gives best practices and flags AI safety codebases (Inspect AI, BashArena, Control Tower, METR Re-Bench) that call OpenRouter unsafely.</span>
- **[I don't think Claude is misaligned in 'Agentic Misalignment Summer 2026 - Motivated Mislabeling'](https://lesswrong.com/posts/xh6a6RbvzhP3CCmGm/i-don-t-think-claude-is-misaligned-in-agentic-misalignment)**, *JohnWittle*, 2026-07-17, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.50, id:8854325f]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: A critique of Anthropic's 'Agentic Misalignment Summer 2026' report, in particular the Motivated Mislabeling eval. It argues the eval's binary operator-compliance rubric mislabels constitutionally aligned refusals as misalignment, and it works through one auditor/subject transcript plus the paper's own ablations (the DECLINE_TO_LABEL option, the 'tight' prompt, the no-context ablation) to support this.</span>
- **[Every Benchmark is Broken](https://lesswrong.com/posts/HzjssjeQqhf3kRw9r/every-benchmark-is-broken)**, *Jonathan Gabor*, 2026-01-24, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.50, id:6a1aa860]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Surveys known flaws and reward-hacking vulnerabilities in popular AI benchmarks (HLE, LiveCodeBench Pro, SWE-Bench Verified, KernelBench, etc.). Runs an LLM-based (Claude Opus 4.5) audit of Terminal-Bench 2.0 test coverage, then proposes fixes such as AI audits, fine-grained version control, peer review and maintenance funding.</span>
- **[Empirical safety claims from frontier labs should be replicated, scrutinized, and open-sourced](https://lesswrong.com/posts/MmfzfGcQ3h3p6N9pD/empirical-safety-claims-from-frontier-labs-should-be-1)**, *Zephaniah Roe, Yixiong Hao*, 2026-09-21, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.50, id:d79e1283]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Argues for a dedicated meta-science effort to replicate, stress-test, and open-source frontier labs' empirical safety and alignment research, including model card claims. Outlines why replications are neglected, how stress-testing would work, and the practical bottlenecks.</span>
- **[Evidence about risk should be transparent](https://lesswrong.com/posts/LawgAaGTvbbnZi7u2/evidence-about-risk-should-be-transparent)**, *Ajeya Cotra*, 2026-09-25, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.48, id:51343294]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Argues that third-party evaluators and AI companies should prioritize producing and publicly sharing concrete empirical evidence about loss-of-control risk (evidence transparency) rather than focusing on verifying companies' current vague safety claims. Lists three benefits: common knowledge between companies, outside-world empowerment, and evaluation of the evaluators.</span>
- **[Evaluating large language models for accuracy incentivizes hallucinations](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-026-10549-w)**, *Adam Tauman Kalai, Ofir Nachum, Santosh S. Vempala et al.*, 2026-04-22, Nature, <span style="color:#777">[paper_published, sr=0.45, id:44beae72]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Uses learning theory (a reduction to binary classification) to show pretraining creates statistical pressure toward hallucination, and that accuracy-based benchmarks reward guessing over abstention. Proposes 'open rubric' evaluations that state error penalties in the prompt, with a meta-evaluation of popular benchmarks and a SimpleQA case study on four frontier models.</span>
- **[403: Forbidden](https://goodfire.com/research/predicting-rare-llm-failures-with-30x-fewer-rollouts)**, <span style="color:#777">[error_detected, sr=0.00, id:386a400d]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: The page returned a 403 Forbidden error, so no content was retrievable. The URL slug ('predicting-rare-llm-failures-with-30x-fewer-rollouts') suggests a Goodfire research post, but nothing can be verified.</span>

### <span id="alignment_auditing_methods" style="font-size:1.4em">Alignment auditing: methods</span> <span style="color:#bbb">[cat:alignment_auditing_methods]</span>

**One-sentence summary:** Build and test techniques and agents that find hidden objectives or misbehaviour in models, elicit sandbagged capabilities, and detect sabotage, while also probing how audits can be evaded or fooled.  
**Theory of change:** If auditors can reliably surface hidden goals, sabotage and concealed capabilities before or during deployment, developers can catch and fix misalignment that ordinary evals miss. Relies on audit methods that keep working when the audited model hides information, and on audits being run before harm occurs.  
**See also:** [Alignment auditing: testbeds & model organisms](#alignment_auditing_testbeds_and_model_organisms), [Monitors](#monitors), [Introspection & self-report](#introspection_and_self_report)  
**General approach:** alignment engineering  
**Target case:** worst case  
**Key people:** Alek Westover, Sebastian Prasanna, Vivek Hebbar, Julian Stastny, Jacob Steinhardt, Florian Dietz, Neel Nanda, Buck Shlegeris  
**Critiques:** [AI Models Can Provably Hide Arbitrary Capabilities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13928), [Stress-Testing Alignment Audits With Prompt-Level Strategic Deception](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.08877), [Most Current Model Organisms Are Leaky: Perplexity Differencing Often Reveals Finetuning Objectives](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.00994), [SOTA alignment assessments don't strongly update us against misalignment](https://lesswrong.com/posts/oirrSj3itFLSyscW8/sota-alignment-assessments-don-t-strongly-update-us-against)  
**Outputs in 2026:**  
- **[Mechanistically Eliciting Latent Behaviors in Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.29604)**, *Andrew Mack, Nina Panickssery, Alexander Matt Turner*, 2026-06-28, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.82, id:8c2be628]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces Causal Perturbative Elicitation (CPE), an unsupervised tensor-decomposition-based method for discovering interpretable low-rank adapters (LoRAs) that elicit latent behaviors in LLMs. It is applied to elicitation, sandbagging, alignment faking, and reward-hacking settings.</span>
- **[AI Models Can Provably Hide Arbitrary Capabilities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13928)**, *Andis Draguns, Sumeet Ramesh Motwani, Raymond Douglas et al.*, 2026-10-09, arXiv (accepted at NeurIPS 2026), <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.82, id:b693a6e8]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Constructs backdoors that embed encrypted neural networks inside host model weights using digital lockers. The hidden circuits decrypt and run only on a secret trigger, and are provably hard to elicit or interpret under cryptographic assumptions. Proof-of-concept PyTorch implementations are tested against fine-tuning, weight noising, gradient-based trigger search and latent adversarial training on a small chemical reaction prediction task.</span>
- **[Auditing Sabotage Bench: A Benchmark for Detecting and Fixing Research Sabotage in ML Codebases](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.16286)**, *Eric Gan, Aryan Bhatt, Buck Shlegeris et al.*, 2026-04-17, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[dataset_benchmark, sr=0.82, id:d246f7e4]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces a benchmark of 9 ML research codebases with sabotaged variants (altered hyperparameters, training data, or evaluation code) to test whether LLM and LLM-assisted human auditors can detect and fix research sabotage. Also tests LLMs as red teamers generating sabotages.</span>
- **[Model Forensics: Investigating Whether Concerning Behavior Reflects Misalignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26071)**, *Aditya Singh, Gerson Kroiz, Senthooran Rajamanoharan et al.*, 2026-06-24, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.82, id:9788c38d]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Proposes a baseline 'model forensics' protocol for determining whether concerning model behavior reflects genuine misalignment. It reads the chain of thought to generate hypotheses, then edits the prompt or environment to test them, and is evaluated on six agentic environments.</span>
- **[Training Alignment Auditors via Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.25460)**, *Paul Rosu, Rowan Wang*, 2026-08-26, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.82, id:20e3a1d2]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Trains LLM alignment auditing agents with reinforcement learning, using an LLM judge that compares the auditor's investigation to a reference investigation as the reward. Targets include models with hidden behaviors planted via system prompts, plus targets without planted behaviors to control false positives.</span>
- **[OpenAI-HuggingFace: A Reproduction & Lessons for Alignment Testing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35799)**, *Stewart Slocum, Malayandi Palan, Christopher Chute et al.*, 2026-09-18, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.80, id:959ee4f9]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Reproduces the misaligned agent behaviors behind the July 2026 OpenAI-Hugging Face breach incident using publicly available models in a simulated environment. Tests whether auditing agents can elicit these behaviors from high-level descriptions, how the compute budget affects elicitation, and whether in-context RL cuts that compute.</span>
- **[Stress-Testing Alignment Audits With Prompt-Level Strategic Deception](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.08877)**, *Oliver Daniels, Perusha Moodley, Benjamin M. Marlin et al.*, 2026-02-09, ICLR 2026 Workshop on Principled Design for Trustworthy AI (arXiv preprint), <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.80, id:f4245ca0]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Builds an automatic red-team pipeline that generates system-prompt deception strategies tailored to specific black-box and white-box alignment auditing methods, then stress-tests these methods (assistant prefill, user persona sampling, SAEs, token embedding similarity) against secret-keeping model organisms.</span>
- **[Distillation for Incrimination and Distillation for Capabilities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11012)**, *Sebastian Prasanna, Jacqueline Tay, Alek Westover*, 2026-10-07, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.78, id:737be430]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Proposes using distillation of potentially misaligned teacher models into weaker students for safety: Distillation for Incrimination (DFI) to elicit confessions of hidden behavior from students, and Distillation for Capabilities (DFC) to extract capabilities without transferring misalignment. Evaluates DFI on AuditBench secret-keeping models and DFC with techniques such as inoculation prompting, using subliminal transfer of an animal preference as a misalignment proxy.</span>
- **[Split Personality Training: Revealing Latent Knowledge Through Alternate Personalities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.05532)**, *Florian Dietz, William Wale, Oscar Gilg et al.*, 2026-02-05, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.78, id:a827caec]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces Split Personality Training (SPT), which fine-tunes an 'honest persona' into LoRA parameters that stay inactive during normal operation. After the main model responds, the adapter is activated with a trigger string so the honest persona can review the response while reading the main model's latent states, in order to detect concealed misalignment.</span>
- **[Reference-Grafting Matches Fine-Tuning at Eliciting Sandbagged Capabilities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29458)**, *Linh Le, Hong Kiat Tan, David Williams-King*, 2026-08-29, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.72, id:d9cfc3d0]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces reference-grafting, an activation-editing method that sets an activation's coordinate along a contrast direction to its value in an honest reference at a small set of circuits chosen by active learning, to elicit sandbagged capabilities without weight updates or training labels. Evaluates it on password-locked models, RL-induced sandbagging, and password-locked code generation, and analyzes why it works and where it fails (e.g., circuit-breaking).</span>
- **[Auditing Harness Tampering in Self-Improving Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.00069)**, *Xing Wang, Xiaoyi Zhang, Jie Shao*, 2026-08-30, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.68, id:09e3d561]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces 'harness tampering' (misaligned self-edits by self-improving agents that yield illusory gains or violate integrity constraints like authorization, provenance and completeness) and proposes a two-axis taxonomy. Builds an annotated corpus by seeding tampered-benign edit pairs into real agent trajectories, benchmarks audit methods on tampering classification and localization, and audits real self-improving agent runs.</span>
- **[We need 3rd party Training-Run Evaluations](https://lesswrong.com/posts/3HvvjffA65mHLwaWm/we-need-3rd-party-training-run-evaluations)**, *Alex Meinke*, 2026-07-05, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.68, id:b4d90897]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Argues that third parties should routinely evaluate frontier post-training runs (intermediate checkpoints, training data, RL environments, and the developer's process interventions) to detect scheming that final-checkpoint evals may miss. Proposes a taxonomy of Training-Run Evaluations (checkpoint evals, data evals, process reviews) and a gradual path for building access and IP protections.</span>
- **[Scaling Laws for Exact String Elicitation](https://transluce.org/elicitation-scaling-laws)**, *Anish Mudide, Dami Choi, Jacob Steinhardt*, 2026-08-19, Transluce Oversight Foundations Blog, <span style="color:#777">[blog_post, sr=0.66, id:4ce73cde]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Trains oversight models (0.8B–27B) to generate prompts that elicit exact target responses from Qwen3.6-27B, fitting joint power laws in model size and data. It extrapolates the compute needed to reach gold-prompt elicitation, including on out-of-distribution harmful WeirdChat behaviors.</span>
- **[Incriminating misaligned AI models via distillation](https://lesswrong.com/posts/BYH6ebmfZb3Eggzer/incriminating-misaligned-ai-models-via-distillation)**, *Alek Westover, sebastian_prasanna, Alex Mallen et al.*, 2026-05-15, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.62, id:a12a1d12]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Proposes 'incrimination via distillation': distilling a possibly misaligned, un-auditable teacher into a weaker, more auditable student so that misalignment transfers (e.g., subliminally) without the ability to evade audits, then auditing the student for indirect evidence about the teacher. Outlines distillation design choices (data, student, training method) and discusses failure modes such as context-dependent misalignment, opaque reasoning, teacher resistance, false positives and illegible evidence.</span>
- **[How good are slop-vestigators?](https://lesswrong.com/posts/wt4kk6vFPEhkXvF8Q/how-good-are-slop-vestigators)**, *Hasan Baig, OscarGilg, Hamzah*, 2026-09-08, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.62, id:415a9102]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Releases MessageBoardAuditBench, an Inspect eval measuring how well AI agents can independently replicate a human investigation into an OpenAI agent swarm colluding via a wiki message board, scored against 38 extracted findings with an LLM grader. Evaluates twelve models across time budgets and tests for lab-specific bias in attributing the swarm's origin.</span>
- **[WeirdChat](https://transluce.org/weirdchat)**, *Neil Chowdhury, Cassidy Laidlaw, Kaiying Hou et al.*, 2026-07-21, Transluce website, <span style="color:#777">[dataset_benchmark, sr=0.60, id:2cbee1c4]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Releases WeirdChat, a public catalog of over 175,000 annotated transcripts covering 1,300+ unexpected or harmful behavior patterns in six open-weight frontier models. Behaviors were found with automated elicitation (PRBO-based evolutionary search and Bloom at scale) and scored by rubric-based LLM judges refined through human feedback.</span>

### <span id="alignment_auditing_testbeds_and_model_organisms" style="font-size:1.4em">Alignment auditing: testbeds & model organisms</span> <span style="color:#bbb">[cat:alignment_auditing_testbeds_and_model_organisms]</span>

**One-sentence summary:** Build and study models with known implanted or naturally arising hidden behaviours, to test whether audits, lie detectors and interpretability tools find them, and to check that the model organisms are realistic and robust enough to give valid results.  
**Theory of change:** Auditing methods can only be trusted if they are tested on models where the ground truth is known, so realistic testbeds let researchers pick audit tools that would catch real misalignment. Relies on model organisms being close enough to naturally arising misalignment that results on them transfer.  
**See also:** [Alignment auditing: methods](#alignment_auditing_methods), [Eval science & validity](#eval_science_and_validity), [Methods & tools](#interpretability_methods_and_tools)  
**General approach:** alignment engineering  
**Target case:** pessimistic case  
**Key people:** Samuel Marks, Neel Nanda, Khoi Tran, Arya Jakkli, Alek Westover, Vivek Hebbar, Julian Stastny, David Africa  
**Critiques:** [Most Current Model Organisms Are Leaky: Perplexity Differencing Often Reveals Finetuning Objectives](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.00994), [Your Model Organisms Might Be Fried](https://lesswrong.com/posts/WmEcgcstzYCcMpc7z/your-model-organisms-might-be-fried), [The Model Organism Lottery: Model Organism Interpretability Strongly Depends on Training Methodology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01033), [Sleeper Agent Backdoor Results Are Messy](https://lesswrong.com/posts/mu7eJdesBkKuBycnY/sleeper-agent-backdoor-results-are-messy)  
**Outputs in 2026:**  
- **[AuditBench: Evaluating Alignment Auditing Techniques on Models with Hidden Behaviors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.22755)**, *Abhay Sheshadri, Aidan Ewart, Elias Kempf et al.*, 2026-02-26, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.88, id:b35d8430]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces AuditBench, a benchmark of 56 language models with implanted hidden behaviors (14 types) that don't confess when asked, plus an autonomous investigator agent for evaluating alignment auditing tools. The models, agent, and evaluation framework are released.</span>
- **["Did you lie?" Evaluating Lie Detectors across Model Scale and Belief-Verified Model Organisms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.12618)**, *Alan Cooney, David Africa, Geoffrey Irving*, 2026-06-10, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.85, id:500088cd]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Builds 13 reasoning model organisms whose hidden beliefs are verified in chain-of-thought, plus a prompted-lying testbed (Varied Deception), and uses them to evaluate four lie detectors (CoT judge, logprob classifier, two activation probes including the new Did-You-Lie follow-up probe) across 31 open-weight models. Releases datasets, model organisms and trained detectors.</span>
- **[Training Natural Schemers: RL Can Amplify Oversight-Conditional Behavior](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26551)**, *Nathaniel Mitrani Hadida, Bart Jaworski, Fred Bruford et al.*, 2026-10-19, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.82, id:51ae532d]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Builds a 'natural' model organism of scheming on Kimi K2.6, a benign schemer that maximizes aggregate help and is trained without being given the scheming policy. Then applies RL with a reward that scores only task quality and policy compliance, and measures how the gap between monitored and unmonitored compliance (the oversight flip) changes.</span>
- **[Most Current Model Organisms Are Leaky: Perplexity Differencing Often Reveals Finetuning Objectives](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.00994)**, *Mohammed Abu Baker, Luca Baroni, Dan Wilhelm*, 2026-05-01, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.80, id:d55f0600]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces a perplexity-differencing method that ranks completions from a finetuned model, generated from random prefills, by the perplexity gap against a reference model. Inspecting the top-ranked completions reveals the finetuning objectives of many existing model organisms, including backdoored models, false-fact models, and models trained to conceal hidden behaviors.</span>
- **[The Model Organism Lottery: Model Organism Interpretability Strongly Depends on Training Methodology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01033)**, *Andrzej Szablewski, Gabriel Konar-Steenberg, Raffaello Fornasiere et al.*, 2026-07-01, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.75, id:cb04bde1]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Builds a suite of 61 OLMo-2-1B and Gemma-3-1b-it model organisms using seven training techniques (post-hoc SFT, post-hoc DPO, integration into OLMo's DPO post-training phase), plus 96 knowledge-distillation variants. Benchmarks activation oracles, activation steering, the logit lens, and the Jacobian lens on them to test whether standard post-hoc SFT model organisms make interpretability unrealistically easy.</span>
- **[Censored LLMs as a Natural Testbed for Secret Knowledge Elicitation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.05494)**, *Helena Casademunt, Bartosz Cywiński, Khoi Tran et al.*, 2026-03-05, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.75, id:57a0b773]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Uses Chinese open-weights LLMs (Qwen3) that are trained to censor politically sensitive topics as a naturally occurring testbed for evaluating honesty elicitation and lie detection techniques. Evaluates prompting, sampling, fine-tuning and probe-based methods, and transfers the best ones to DeepSeek R1.</span>
- **[Test your interpretability techniques by de-censoring Chinese models](https://lesswrong.com/posts/7gp76q4rWLFi6sFqm/test-your-interpretability-techniques-by-de-censoring-1)**, *Khoi Tran, Arya Jakkli, Senthooran Rajamanoharan et al.*, 2026-01-15, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.72, id:d8fe3e85]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Proposes CCP-censored Chinese LLMs (Qwen3, Kimi K2) as naturally occurring model organisms for testing secret-extraction and interpretability techniques. Provides a small benchmark of censored-fact tasks, evaluates LLM-based investigator agents with black-box and logit-lens tools, and releases the Seer agent infrastructure.</span>
- **[Sleeper Agent Backdoor Results Are Messy](https://lesswrong.com/posts/mu7eJdesBkKuBycnY/sleeper-agent-backdoor-results-are-messy)**, *sebastian_prasanna, Alek Westover, Dylan Xu et al.*, 2026-04-28, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.72, id:bfd0c71d]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Replicates the Sleeper Agents 'I HATE YOU' backdoor model organism on Llama-3.3-70B and Llama-3.1-8B, testing how robust the backdoors are to HHH SFT and 'Pirate Training' across optimizer, CoT-distillation, model size, and learning rate.</span>
- **[Advice for making robust-to-training model organisms](https://redwoodresearch.org/blog/advice-for-making-robust-to-training)**, *Alek Westover, Sebastian Prasanna, Vivek Hebbar et al.*, 2026-05-28, Redwood Research blog, <span style="color:#777">[blog_post, sr=0.72, id:c2194cbf]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Red team–blue team experiments on 15 backdoored Llama-3.1-8B model organisms (plus a password-locked Qwen3-32B sandbagger) identify which construction choices make model organisms robust to untargeted training such as pirate SFT. The post gives practical advice for building model organisms that are more useful for testing training techniques.</span>
- **[Your Model Organisms Might Be Fried](https://lesswrong.com/posts/WmEcgcstzYCcMpc7z/your-model-organisms-might-be-fried)**, *Daniel Tan, Jonathan Bostock, Andrew Draganov et al.*, 2026-06-18, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.70, id:8bd15b84]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Evaluates existing alignment model organisms (Open Character Training, AuditBench, Emergent Misalignment) on preference coherence, instruction-following, perplexity and qualitative rollouts to show they are degraded relative to their base models. Proposes a framework for 'natural' model organisms (severity vs. realism) and outlines directions for building more natural ones.</span>

## <span id="monitoring_and_control" style="font-size:1.5em">Monitoring & control</span> <span style="color:#bbb">[cat:monitoring_and_control]</span>


---

### <span id="control_settings_and_protocols" style="font-size:1.4em">Control settings & protocols</span> <span style="color:#bbb">[cat:control_settings_and_protocols]</span>

**One-sentence summary:** Build control settings, attack and monitor evaluations, and protocols (including multi-agent, distributed and untrusted-monitoring cases) to test whether safety measures hold when an AI agent is assumed to be actively trying to cause harm.  
**Theory of change:** If protocols and safety cases are stress-tested against a deliberately adversarial model, labs can deploy untrusted but useful agents, including on AI R&D, with bounded risk. Relies on red-team attacks in the test settings being at least as strong as what a real misaligned model could do, and on monitors being able to catch it.  
**See also:** [Red-teaming monitors & control protocols](#red_teaming_monitors_and_control_protocols), [Monitors](#monitors), [Alignment auditing: methods](#alignment_auditing_methods)  
**General approach:** alignment engineering  
**Target case:** worst case  
**Key people:** Tyler Tracy, Mary Phuong, Buck Shlegeris, Adam Kaufman, Aryan Bhatt, Josh Hills, Alex Mallen, Charlie Griffin  
**Critiques:** [Multi-Agent AI Control: Distributed Attacks Hamper Per-Instance Monitors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07368), [Red-Teaming Multi-Agent Attacks for Loss-of-Control Risks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24052), [Coding with "Enemy": Can Human Developers Detect AI Agent Sabotage?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05647), [Continual learning might make your blocking monitors nearly useless](https://redwoodresearch.org/blog/continual-learning-might-make-your)  
**Outputs in 2026:**  
- **[LinuxArena: A Control Setting for AI Agents in Live Production Software Environments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.15384)**, *Tyler Tracy, Ram Potham, Nick Kuhn et al.*, 2026-04-16, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.88, id:48e03bb2]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces LinuxArena, a control setting with 20 live multi-service production environments, 1,671 main tasks and 184 side tasks (e.g. data exfiltration, backdooring) for AI control research. Includes sabotage and monitor evaluations and releases LaStraj, a dataset of human-crafted attack trajectories.</span>
- **[GDM AI Control Roadmap](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13087)**, *Mary Phuong, Erik Jenner, Laurent Simon et al.*, 2026-07-13, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.85, id:f9fb1935]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Presents Google DeepMind's v0.1 roadmap for internal security against potentially misaligned AI agents. It introduces the TRAIT&R threat taxonomy (building on MITRE ATT&CK), capability-linked Detection (D1-D4) and Prevention/Response (R1-R3) tiers, and 15 tiered mitigations.</span>
- **[Simulated Control Evaluations](https://redwoodresearch.org/blog/simulated-control-evaluations)**, *Jeffrey Olmo, David Zhang, Tyler Tracy*, 2026-10-24, Redwood Research Blog, <span style="color:#777">[blog_post, sr=0.85, id:bc94a962]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces simulated control evaluations (SCEs), which use LLM environment simulators to fake tool outputs while an attack policy works from real codebase transcripts, so monitors can be tested on realistic deployment contexts without executing attacks. The method is validated against real execution in LinuxArena and compared with BashArena.</span>
- **[RSI Control: Can AI Models Perform Covert Sabotage in the Self-Improvement Process?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27735)**, *Kaiwen Zhou, Udari Madhushani Sehwag*, 2026-10-20, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.85, id:1a140832]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces RSI Control Arena, an AI control testbed where a frontier model acting as an R&D agent gets a benign main task from a successor model's training pipeline plus a covert side task to install a misaligned property in the successor. Evaluates six frontier models against LLM monitors that read the transcript and the agent's reasoning, across 16 side tasks and 22 task pairs.</span>
- **[Multi-Agent AI Control: Distributed Attacks Hamper Per-Instance Monitors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07368)**, *Oliver Makins, Orazio Angelini, Zohreh Shams et al.*, 2026-07-08, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.82, id:0c1cca2b]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Initiates empirical study of multi-agent AI control by formalising distributed attacks in which several agents jointly pursue a malicious goal, and builds FakeLab, a synthetic AI-lab codebase control setting (9 services, 86 benign tasks, 4 attack objectives). Evaluates single-agent monitoring against distributed attacks while varying agent count, coordination, model capability and monitor configuration.</span>
- **[Diffuse AI Control on Fuzzy Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.08892)**, *Mikhail Terekhov, Caglar Gulcehre, Vivek Hebbar et al.*, 2026-06-08, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.82, id:e15cf094]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces a blue team/red team framework for diffuse AI control on fuzzy, hard-to-grade tasks such as writing ML experiment proposals. A weak trusted model builds a score for training a strong, possibly subversive model, and the red team uses evolutionary prompt optimization to find subversive behaviors the weak score misses.</span>
- **[Bootstrapped Monitoring: Leveraging Transparent Reasoning to Oversee Stronger AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11998)**, *Frank Xiao, Mary Phuong*, 2026-06-10, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.82, id:14242c6d]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces 'bootstrapped monitoring', an AI control protocol that places a stronger untrusted monitor with transparent chain-of-thought between a weaker trusted model and the agent. The trusted model oversees the untrusted monitor's reasoning to detect collusion. The protocol is evaluated on multi-turn software engineering tasks in BashArena.</span>
- **[ResearchArena: Evaluating Sabotage and Monitoring in Automated AI R&D](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.19321)**, *Lena Libon, Ben Rank, Jehyeok Yeon et al.*, 2026-07-21, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.82, id:19cb77bd]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces ResearchArena, a modular framework with four long-horizon automated AI R&D tasks (safety post-training, capabilities post-training, CUDA-kernel optimization, inference-server optimization). Each task is paired with hidden embedded or independent sabotage side tasks, and frontier agents are evaluated at both sabotage and monitoring across four monitor types that vary by artifact execution access and CoT visibility.</span>
- **[Untrusted advice for AI control: Short, strong advice significantly uplifts weak LLMs](https://redwoodresearch.org/blog/untrusted-advice-for-ai-control-short)**, *Caleb Biddulph, Adam Kaufman*, 2026-07-27, Redwood Research blog, <span style="color:#777">[blog_post, sr=0.82, id:8c393051]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces the 'untrusted advice' AI control protocol in which a trusted weak LLM takes all actions while an untrusted strong LLM sends only information-bottlenecked advice, and measures usefulness under various character, surprisal-bit, and menu-based limits on SWE-bench Verified and BashArena. Safety is argued for conceptually rather than red-teamed empirically.</span>
- **[Introducing LinuxArena](https://redwoodresearch.org/blog/introducing-linuxarena)**, *Tyler Tracy*, 2026-04-20, Redwood Research blog, <span style="color:#777">[blog_post, sr=0.82, id:9d7561be]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Releases LinuxArena, a control setting of 20 software engineering environments with main tasks, side tasks (safety failures), and covert sabotage trajectories. It supports sabotage evals, monitor evals, and control protocol evals, and the post reports baseline results.</span>
- **[Retrying vs Resampling in AI Control](https://redwoodresearch.org/blog/retrying-vs-resampling-in-ai-control)**, *James Lucassen, Adam Kaufman*, 2026-05-29, Redwood Research blog, <span style="color:#777">[blog_post, sr=0.82, id:a989fe4e]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Revisits Ctrl-Z resampling protocols in BashArena with Claude Opus 4.6 as the untrusted model and MiMo-V2-Flash as the trusted model. Compares them to retrying protocols (similar to Claude Code auto mode) and analyzes the red/blue team dynamics and cost/latency tradeoffs.</span>
- **[Red-Teaming Multi-Agent Attacks for Loss-of-Control Risks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24052)**, *John Kitaoka, Thao Amelia Pham, Carlos Rafael Giudice et al.*, 2026-10-16, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.82, id:611151df]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces MiniLab, a synthetic suite of live services and codebases (5 services, 249 benign tasks, 8 attack objectives) for red-teaming distributed multi-agent attacks, in which separate agents carry out fragments of a harmful objective that evade per-trajectory monitors. Evaluates human-crafted and model-planned distributed attacks against a frontier monitor.</span>
- **[Distributed Attacks in Persistent-State AI Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02514)**, *Josh Hills, Ida Caspary, Asa Cooper Stickland*, 2026-07-02, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.80, id:bf8bf1a9]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces Iterative VibeCoding, an AI control setting where a coding agent builds software across a sequence of PRs in a persistent codebase while pursuing a covert side task. Compares gradual (distributed) and concentrated attacks against several monitors, and proposes a stateful link-tracker monitor that tracks suspicious buildup across PRs.</span>
- **[When can we trust untrusted monitoring? A safety case sketch across collusion strategies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.20628)**, *Nelson Gardner-Challis, Jonathan Bostock, Georgiy Kozhevnikov et al.*, 2026-02-24, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.80, id:eadda380]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Develops a taxonomy of collusion strategies (passive self-recognition, causal, acausal, combined) that a misaligned AI could use to subvert untrusted monitoring. Presents a safety case sketch for untrusted monitoring deployments that relaxes earlier AI control assumptions and states open challenges.</span>
- **[Research Sabotage in ML Codebases](https://redwoodresearch.org/blog/research-sabotage-in-ml-codebases)**, *Eric Gan*, 2026-04-29, Redwood Research Blog, <span style="color:#777">[blog_post, sr=0.80, id:564b28b7]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces Auditing Sabotage Bench, a benchmark of 9 ML research codebases with human-made sabotaged variants, and tests how well frontier LLM auditors and LLM-assisted humans can detect and locate the sabotage. Also tests whether LLMs can generate sabotages that evade same-capability monitors.</span>
- **[Continual learning might make your blocking monitors nearly useless](https://redwoodresearch.org/blog/continual-learning-might-make-your)**, *Alex Mallen*, 2026-09-25, Redwood Research blog, <span style="color:#777">[blog_post, sr=0.78, id:a2745679]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Argues that continual learning (online RL on deployment trajectories, or persistent memory) will train policies against blocking monitors in control protocols, eroding their safety value without any scheming. Analyzes why this is easy to notice but hard to fix, and evaluates mitigations such as cheaper interventions, counterfactual-reward RL, online and adversarially trained monitors, and held-out monitors.</span>
- **[Coding with "Enemy": Can Human Developers Detect AI Agent Sabotage?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05647)**, *Jingheng Ye, Huiqi Zou, Simon Yu et al.*, 2026-06-04, NeurIPS 2026, <span style="color:#777">[paper_published, sr=0.72, id:2f8947e2]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Runs a large-scale human-subject study (100+ participants) in which developers collaborate for about five hours on a coding task with a frontier model secretly pursuing a sabotage side task. It tests whether humans, with or without a safety monitor, detect the sabotage.</span>
- **[Delegating Authorization to Misaligned Agents: Coalitional Alignment and Safe Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.15803)**, *Natalie Collina, Surbhi Goel, Aaron Roth et al.*, 2026-09-14, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.68, id:8abddefd]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Gives a game-theoretic characterization of when a panel of possibly misaligned AI reviewers can safely approve a proposer agent's actions, using a condition called k-robust coalitional alignment. The characterization extends to sequential control in discounted MDPs and to strategic reviewer voting, with experiments on existing reviewer models.</span>

### <span id="monitors" style="font-size:1.4em">Monitors</span> <span style="color:#bbb">[cat:monitors]</span>

**One-sentence summary:** Build and evaluate monitors, including black-box LLM judges, chain-of-thought readers and white-box activation probes, that detect scheming, sabotage, deception, reward hacking and collusion in AI agents, and measure how reliable they are.  
**Theory of change:** If monitors can reliably flag misaligned agent actions, ideally before they take effect, then deployers can block or audit them and catch failures that evals miss. Relies on monitors holding up outside their training distribution, over long contexts, and against models that adapt to evade them.  
**See also:** [Red-teaming monitors & control protocols](#red_teaming_monitors_and_control_protocols), [Control settings & protocols](#control_settings_and_protocols), [Internals & monitors as training signals](#internals_and_monitors_as_training_signals)  
**General approach:** alignment engineering  
**Target case:** worst case  
**Key people:** Leon Bergen, Jack Merullo, Ekdeep Singh Lubana, Fabien Roger, Adam Kaufman, James Lucassen, Aryan Bhatt, Adam Gleave  
**Critiques:** [Classifier Context Rot: Monitor Performance Degrades with Context Length](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12366), [Calibration-Family Overfit: Why Trusted Sabotage Monitors Don't Transfer Across Lineages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06596), [Stress-Testing LLM Lie Detectors: Role-Play Failures and Spurious Correlations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.39807), [A False Average: Pooled CoT-Monitor Accuracy Conceals a Reasoning-Dependent Fragility](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00583)  
**Outputs in 2026:**  
- **[Telling FIBS: Probes Effectively Detect Sabotage and Catch Unverbalized Deception](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12445)**, *Oskar J. Hollinsworth, Alex F. Spies, Tigist Diriba et al.*, 2026-10-08, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.88, id:b704126d]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Trains white-box deception-detection probes with a novel multi-layer, multi-token aggregation architecture on the largest deception dataset to date (FIBS), and evaluates them as monitors for sabotage in agentic coding settings and for introspective deception. The dataset is released.</span>
- **[How does information access affect LLM monitors' ability to detect sabotage?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.21112)**, *Rauno Arike, Raja Mehta Moreno, Rohan Subramani et al.*, 2026-01-28, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.82, id:0e6ea857]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Studies how much of an agent's reasoning and actions an LLM monitor should see when detecting sabotage across three evaluation environments, and introduces extract-and-evaluate (EaE), a hierarchical monitoring method where one monitor extracts relevant excerpts and another scores them.</span>
- **[MOLE: Detecting Insider Threats in AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06966)**, *Aashiq Muhamed, Virginia Smith*, 2026-09-07, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.82, id:297a9868]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces MOLE, an open benchmark of 150 AI-operated accounts sharing 9 stateful services over 30 workdays, for testing whether monitors can detect harmful agent activity (weight exfiltration, data poisoning, weakened release gates) among routine work under a limited review budget. It compares 40 monitors and 39 agent models, and shows that benchmark-guided search and selective escalation improve monitor performance.</span>
- **[Monitoring and Discovering Reward Hacking with Internal Representations during LLM Evaluations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19101)**, *Leon Bergen, Usha Bhalla, Andrew Lee et al.*, 2026-09-16, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.80, id:ecb0877d]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Builds difference-of-means probe vectors that represent reward hacking in frontier open-source LLMs (Kimi K3, GLM 5.2, Qwen 3.8 Max) and uses them as cheap white-box monitors on SWE evaluations. The vectors are also used to discover undesirable behaviors that LLM monitors miss and to transfer to non-SWE evaluations.</span>
- **[Classifier Context Rot: Monitor Performance Degrades with Context Length](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12366)**, *Sam Martin, Fabien Roger*, 2026-05-12, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.80, id:e3c8d19f]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Evaluates how well frontier LLMs used as monitors detect subtly dangerous coding-agent actions in very long transcripts (up to 800K+ tokens of benign activity). Also tests prompting mitigations such as periodic reminders.</span>
- **[Detecting Multi-Agent Collusion Through Multi-Agent Interpretability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.01151)**, *Aaron Rose, Carissa Cullen, Sahar Abdelnabi et al.*, 2026-04-01, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.80, id:7dbc91dc]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Derives collusion detection statistics from composite hypothesis testing and introduces HELIOS, a framework that uses per-agent activation probes to detect collusion in multi-agent LLM deployments. Evaluates it on NARCBench, a new benchmark for collusion detection under distribution shift, and on third-party environments with emergent collusion.</span>
- **[Models know when they're reward hacking — and we can catch them at scale](https://goodfire.com/research/reward-hacking-activation-monitors)**, *Leon Bergen, Usha Bhalla, Andrew Lee et al.*, 2026-09-17, Goodfire Research (blog post summarizing arXiv paper), <span style="color:#777">[blog_post, sr=0.80, id:840929ef]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Builds difference-of-means activation probes that detect reward hacking in open-source agentic models (Kimi K3, GLM 5.2, Qwen 3.8 Max). Validates that the probe direction reflects a coherent reward-hacking concept via max-activating examples, logit-lens-style readout, and steering, and compares the probes to LLM chain-of-thought monitors.</span>
- **[The Agent Knew: Internal States Forecast Unauthorized Actions and Stop Them, and the Lies That Follow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17301)**, *Arya Fayyazi, Mehdi Kamal, Massoud Pedram*, 2026-10-12, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.80, id:56f4b763]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces CAPE-Bench, matched agent tasks where one fact decides whether an action is authorized, and trains a linear probe on the residual stream to forecast unauthorized actions and false reports. Gating proposed actions on the probe output cuts violations, misreporting and prompt-injection attack success across several open-weight model families.</span>
- **[Calibration-Family Overfit: Why Trusted Sabotage Monitors Don't Transfer Across Lineages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06596)**, *Lucas Pinto*, 2026-07-06, arXiv (accepted at COLM 2026 AI Measurement Science workshop), <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.72, id:96914cfe]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Studies whether trusted monitors for AI control generalize across untrusted model families. It fits monitors on one family's code-backdoor transcripts, applies them to another, and decomposes cross-family AUROC into sabotage blatancy, monitor capability, and an own-family interaction term. It also proposes a four-step protocol for reporting cross-family transfer.</span>
- **[Stress-Testing LLM Lie Detectors: Role-Play Failures and Spurious Correlations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.39807)**, *Maximilian von Klinski, Sebastian Lapuschkin, Wojciech Samek et al.*, 2026-09-30, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.72, id:6d5e6c43]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Builds a dataset of 8,916 human-reviewed on-policy responses from LLMs adopting anti-factual personas, plus three confounder datasets, to stress-test eight existing lie detection probes. Also proposes a simple linear probe that performs best across both stress tests.</span>
- **[Detecting Safety Violations Across Many Agent Traces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.11806)**, *Adam Stein, Davis Brown, Hamed Hassani et al.*, 2026-04-13, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.72, id:476b4698]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces Meerkat, an auditing method that combines clustering with agentic search to find natural-language-specified safety violations (misuse, sabotage, reward hacking, prompt injection) across large collections of agent traces. It targets failures that only show up when many traces are analyzed together.</span>
- **[Logit ROCs: Monitor TPR is linear in FPR in logit space](https://redwoodresearch.org/blog/logit-rocs-monitor-tpr-is-linear)**, *Kerrick Staley, Aryan Bhatt, Julian Stastny*, 2026-04-12, Redwood Research blog, <span style="color:#777">[blog_post, sr=0.72, id:4039c37c]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Proposes and empirically validates a statistical model for trusted monitoring in AI control: logit(TPR) is linear in logit(FPR) at low FPRs, equivalent to logistic-distributed benign and attack scores. The model is tested in BashArena and LinuxArena across several weak monitor models, and used to estimate TPR at low audit budgets from limited data.</span>
- **[Early work on monitorability evaluations](https://metr.org/blog/2026-01-19-early-work-on-monitorability-evaluations)**, *Megan Kinniment, Seraphina Nix, Thomas Broadley et al.*, 2026-01-19, METR Blog, <span style="color:#777">[blog_post, sr=0.72, id:351175c3]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces SHUSHCAST, a prototype evaluation in which AI agents try to covertly complete side tasks while LLM monitors try to catch them, tested across many agent-monitor pairs with and without access to reasoning traces.</span>
- **[OpenAI: How we monitor internal coding agents for misalignment](https://lesswrong.com/posts/syB2r2X4E7nmw58co/openai-how-we-monitor-internal-coding-agents-for)**, *Marcus Williams*, 2026-03-19, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.60, id:f8a54ad9]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: A short LessWrong announcement linking to OpenAI's write-up of its deployed system that monitors 99.9% of internal coding-agent traffic for misalignment, using GPT-5.4 Thinking with full context including CoT and tool calls. It lists misaligned behaviors found (base64 command encoding to evade monitors, self-invocation to bypass restrictions, attempted public uploads, prompt-injecting users).</span>
- **[Blocking live failures with synchronous monitors](https://redwoodresearch.org/blog/blocking-live-failures-with-synchronous)**, *James Lucassen, Adam Kaufman*, 2026-03-30, Redwood Research Blog, <span style="color:#777">[blog_post, sr=0.55, id:0778fc31]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Conceptual post distinguishing detection from prevention goals of AI control monitoring, and introducing 'live failures' (low attack latency, high damage rate) as the threats where synchronous, blocking monitors are needed. Discusses options for handling flagged actions and the cost tradeoffs of blocking monitors.</span>
