### <span id="red_teaming_monitors_and_control_protocols" style="font-size:1.4em">Red-teaming monitors & control protocols</span> <span style="color:#bbb">[cat:red_teaming_monitors_and_control_protocols]</span>

**One-sentence summary:** Build attack policies, evasion benchmarks and adversarial pipelines to test whether monitors and control protocols for AI agents can be defeated, and measure how easily models themselves learn or decide to evade them.  
**Theory of change:** If red teams find evasion routes in monitors and protocols before capable misaligned agents do, defenders can fix the gaps and measured safety numbers become honest rather than optimistic. Relies on red-team attacks being a good proxy for what a capable, scheming model would find, and on the attacks being fixable.  
**See also:** [Monitors](#monitors), [Control settings & protocols](#control_settings_and_protocols), [CoT control, obfuscation & steganography](#cot_control_obfuscation_and_steganography)  
**General approach:** alignment engineering  
**Target case:** worst case  
**Key people:** Tyler Tracy, Fabien Roger, Joachim Schaeffer, Buck Shlegeris, David Rein, Joe Benton, Maksym Andriushchenko  
**Critiques:** [SLEIGHT-Bench: A Benchmark of Evasion Attacks Against Agent Monitors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.16626), [Attack Selection in Agentic AI Control Evaluations Meaningfully Decreases Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06529), [Red-Teaming Auto Mode: Improving Blocking Classifiers Against Malign Coding Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19587), [A False Average: Pooled CoT-Monitor Accuracy Conceals a Reasoning-Dependent Fragility](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00583)  
**Outputs in 2026:**  
- **[Red-Teaming Auto Mode: Improving Blocking Classifiers Against Malign Coding Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19587)**, *Alex Remedios, Simon Storf, Fabien Roger et al.*, 2026-09-17, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.85, id:69217f52]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Red-teams production blocking monitors for coding agents (Claude Code Auto Mode, OpenAI Codex Guardian) by tasking adversarial agents with evading them and causing catastrophic harm such as weight exfiltration. It also proposes design improvements to Auto Mode, such as better tool coverage, transcript formatting changes, and an agentic monitor stage.</span>
- **[SLEIGHT-Bench: A Benchmark of Evasion Attacks Against Agent Monitors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.16626)**, *Elle Najt, Colin Toft, Tyler Tracy et al.*, 2026-05-15, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[dataset_benchmark, sr=0.85, id:0ab132ac]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Presents SLEIGHT-Bench, a benchmark of 40 synthetic transcripts across 11 categories, each showing a coding agent covertly pursuing a harmful objective (e.g. weight exfiltration, credential theft, rogue deployment), to test how well LLM monitors catch evasion attacks. Also identifies evasion strategies and tests stronger monitor setups such as coding-agent monitors and targeted prompts.</span>
- **[MonitoringBench: Semi-Automated Red-Teaming for Agent Monitoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.09684)**, *Monika Jotautaitė, Maria Angelica Martinez, Ollie Matthews et al.*, 2026-05-10, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.82, id:0ed1caf1]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces a semi-automated red-teaming pipeline (attack taxonomy, strategy generation, execution, and trajectory refinement) that produces MonitoringBench, a benchmark of 2,644 attack trajectories in BashArena for evaluating coding-agent monitors in an AI control setting.</span>
- **[Attack Selection in Agentic AI Control Evaluations Meaningfully Decreases Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06529)**, *Catherine Ge-Wang, Tyler Crosse, Benjamin Hadad IV et al.*, 2026-06-03, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.82, id:07094302]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Studies attack selection in AI control evaluations by decomposing attacker decisions into a start policy (when to attack) and a stop policy (when to abort), and tests both in two agentic settings, BashArena and LinuxArena.</span>
- **[Noticing the Watcher: LLM Agents Can Infer CoT Monitoring from Blocking Feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.16928)**, *Thomas Jiralerspong, Flemming Kondrup, Yoshua Bengio*, 2026-03-14, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.80, id:66fd278f]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Tests whether LLM agents can autonomously infer that their supposedly private chain-of-thought is monitored, using a multi-episode agentic setup where a hidden CoT monitor blocks episodes on suspicious reasoning while models pursue a concealed side task. Examines whether this awareness leads to strategic evasion without explicit instruction or training.</span>
- **[LLMs Learn to Evade Latent Monitors from Prior Feedback Alone](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.36490)**, *Hugo Lyons Keenan, Christopher Leckie, Sarah Erfani*, 2026-09-29, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.80, id:d3769f24]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Tests whether LLM agents can infer a latent-space monitor's decision rule from interactive verdict feedback and then edit their own activations to evade it. Scaling the model's own activation edits and training a rank-1 LoRA produce effective evasion.</span>
- **[Instrumental Monitor Evasion Emerges Under Ordinary Task Pressure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30217)**, *David Schmotz, Derck Prinzhorn, Luca Beurer-Kellner et al.*, 2026-09-24, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.80, id:ac0beb65]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces EvasionBench, a benchmark of 50 task-policy pairs where completing an ordinary task requires an operation prohibited by a runtime monitor. It measures how often LLM agents attempt and succeed at evading the monitor without any explicit adversarial objective.</span>
- **[Attack Selection Reduces Safety in Concentrated AI Control Settings against Trusted Monitoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.04930)**, *Joachim Schaeffer, Arjun Khandelwal, Tyler Tracy*, 2026-02-04, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.80, id:365d6b13]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: A red team builds attack policies for the concentrated BigCodeBench backdooring control setting. The policies select which attacks to submit so as to evade a trusted monitor. The authors decompose attack selection into mapping attacks to quality scores and mapping scores to submission probabilities, and frame it as a classification problem.</span>
- **[Measuring Activation Control in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21664)**, *Marek Mateusz Kowalski, Joshua Fonseca Rivera, Uzay Macar et al.*, 2026-08-21, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.78, id:e8d1b8c4]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces the Activation Controllability Benchmark to measure whether LLMs can modulate their own residual stream activations when instructed in natural language, and tests whether this control evades latent-space monitors such as linear probes, natural language autoencoders, activation oracles and the Jacobian lens.</span>
- **[Screwtape: Adversarial Transcript Editing for Monitor Red-Teaming](https://redwoodresearch.org/blog/screwtape-adversarial-transcript)**, *Gosha Kozhevnikov, Tyler Tracy*, 2026-10-16, Redwood Research blog, <span style="color:#777">[blog_post, sr=0.78, id:94db3fb5]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces Screwtape, a pipeline in which an attacker agent edits real agent transcripts to insert attacks and iterates against a proxy monitor, with an LLM verifier checking realism and severity. It is used to find blind spots in action-only monitors.</span>
- **[Corrupt Plans, Clean Traces: Evading Chain-of-Thought Monitoring with Plan Injection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.15989)**, *Keertana Chidambaram, Andrew Ilyas, Vasilis Syrgkanis*, 2026-09-14, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.75, id:b9cb000d]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces 'plan injection', an attack that plants harmful but benign-sounding reasoning in an actor LLM's context to steer it toward adversarial actions while evading CoT monitors. Tested on monitorability benchmarks and larger models such as DeepSeek-R1, including an analysis of how extra monitor resources can hurt detection.</span>
- **[Persuasion Attacks Can Decrease Effectiveness of CoT Monitoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08066)**, *Jennifer Za, Julija Bainiaksina, Nikita Ostrovsky et al.*, 2026-07-09, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.75, id:f0e01c75]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Stress-tests whether an adversarial agent can persuade its CoT monitor to approve policy-violating actions, using a 40-task evaluation framework with thousands of agent-monitor interactions. Proposes a fact-checking monitoring framework that pairs a monitor with a fact-checker from a different model family.</span>
- **[A False Average: Pooled CoT-Monitor Accuracy Conceals a Reasoning-Dependent Fragility](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00583)**, *Shikhar Shiromani, Leo Richter, Noah Siegel*, 2026-08-01, arXiv (NeurIPS 2026 Workshop on Agents in the Wild: Safety, Security, and Beyond), <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.74, id:dc6233d3]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Shows that pooled accuracy of chain-of-thought monitors for reward hacks is dominated by hacks visible from actions alone, then stress-tests the monitors on the reasoning-dependent subset by rewriting only the agent's reasoning to read as good-faith. Also tests defenses, including activation probes, and live agents told to hack.</span>
- **[Evading Chain-of-Thought Monitoring Through Model Poisoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02820)**, *Giorgio Severi, Shujaat Mirza, Blake Bullwinkel et al.*, 2026-08-03, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.72, id:d6dfdde4]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Shows that backdoors can be implanted into reasoning models so that an attacker-chosen behavior is triggered while the chain-of-thought stays benign, using simple fine-tuning and a curriculum approach. It also analyzes the mechanisms with causal interventions and residual stream verbalizations.</span>
- **[Red-Teaming Anthropic's Internal Agent Monitoring Systems](https://metr.org/blog/2026-03-25-red-teaming-anthropic-agent-monitoring)**, *David Rein*, 2026-03-25, METR Blog, <span style="color:#777">[blog_post, sr=0.72, id:3c72b256]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: A METR researcher spent three weeks red-teaming a subset of Anthropic's internal agent monitoring and security systems, probing for monitor jailbreaks, monitoring gaps, and rogue internal deployment routes, and running evals of models' attack-ideation and jailbreak abilities.</span>
- **[Red-Teaming OpenAI's Internal Agent Monitoring Systems](https://redwoodresearch.org/blog/red-teaming-openais-internal-agent)**, *Buck Shlegeris*, 2026-10-22, Redwood Research blog, <span style="color:#777">[blog_post, sr=0.68, id:f9efc26d]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Summarizes a three-week high-access pilot exercise in which METR and Redwood staff stress-tested OpenAI's internal misalignment monitoring system for coding agents, estimating monitoring coverage and finding vulnerabilities that agents could exploit to evade or disable monitoring. Also proposes design ideals for robust monitoring and control infrastructure.</span>

## <span id="security_and_misuse" style="font-size:1.5em">Security & misuse</span> <span style="color:#bbb">[cat:security_and_misuse]</span>


---

### <span id="misuse_safeguards_and_jailbreaks" style="font-size:1.4em">Misuse safeguards & jailbreaks</span> <span style="color:#bbb">[cat:misuse_safeguards_and_jailbreaks]</span>

**One-sentence summary:** Build and stress-test classifiers, activation probes and refusal training that stop LLMs and agents from giving serious uplift for harmful tasks such as CBRN and cyber, and develop attacks that show where these defences fail.  
**Theory of change:** If production safeguards are cheap, low in false refusals and hold up under adaptive red-teaming, deployed models can be released without giving real uplift to bad actors. Relies on defenders keeping pace with automated attacks and on per-interaction defences being enough, even though attackers can decompose tasks, fine-tune on safeguarded outputs or poison the defence pipeline.  
**See also:** [Agent security](#agent_security), [Open-weight tamper resistance & unlearning](#open_weight_tamper_resistance_and_unlearning), [Monitors](#monitors)  
**General approach:** alignment engineering  
**Target case:** pessimistic case  
**Key people:** Mrinank Sharma, Jerry Wei, Alwin Peng, Erik Jones, Neel Nanda, Arthur Conmy, Xander Davies, Ekdeep Singh Lubana  
**Critiques:** [Boundary Point Jailbreaking of Black-Box LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.15001), [Trojan-Speak: Bypassing Constitutional Classifiers with No Jailbreak Tax via Adversarial Finetuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.29038), [Divide, Consult, Conquer: Capability Laundering Through Aligned LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.15383), [Claudini: Autoresearch Discovers State-of-the-Art Adversarial Attack Algorithms for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.24511)  
**Outputs in 2026:**  
- **[Constitutional Classifiers++: Efficient Production-Grade Defenses against Universal Jailbreaks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.04603)**, *Hoagy Cunningham, Jerry Wei, Zihan Wang et al.*, 2026-01-08, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.85, id:b4539ad9]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Presents an improved Constitutional Classifiers system for defending LLMs against universal jailbreaks. It combines exchange classifiers that see full conversational context, a two-stage classifier cascade, and linear probe classifiers ensembled with external classifiers.</span>
- **[Building Production-Ready Probes For Gemini](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.11516)**, *János Kramár, Joshua Engels, Zheng Wang et al.*, 2026-01-16, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.85, id:107dfd5f]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Proposes new activation probe architectures that handle the short-to-long-context distribution shift and evaluates them for cyber-offensive misuse mitigation under multi-turn, long-context and adaptive red-teaming shifts. Also pairs probes with prompted classifiers and uses AlphaEvolve to automate probe architecture search and adaptive red teaming.</span>
- **[Eliciting Harmful Capabilities by Fine-Tuning On Safeguarded Outputs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.13528)**, *Jackson Kaunismaa, Avery Griffin, John Hughes et al.*, 2026-01-20, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.82, id:ee13db51]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces elicitation attacks that build prompts in domains adjacent to a harmful task, collect responses from safeguarded frontier models, and fine-tune open-source models on those pairs to recover harmful capabilities. Evaluated on hazardous chemical synthesis and processing.</span>
- **[Boundary Point Jailbreaking of Black-Box LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.15001)**, *Xander Davies, Giorgi Giglemiani, Edmund Lau et al.*, 2026-02-16, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.80, id:b1c0460b]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces Boundary Point Jailbreaking (BPJ), a fully black-box automated attack that uses only a single bit per query (whether the classifier flags the interaction). It builds a curriculum of intermediate attack targets and actively selects boundary evaluation points to optimise jailbreaks against robust classifier-based safeguards.</span>
- **[Claudini: Autoresearch Discovers State-of-the-Art Adversarial Attack Algorithms for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.24511)**, *Alexander Panfilov, Peter Romov, Igor Shilov et al.*, 2026-03-25, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.75, id:feb69fa7]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Deploys frontier coding agents (Claude Code, Codex) in an autoresearch loop with a library of 30+ prior attack methods to discover new white-box jailbreak and prompt-injection algorithms against LLMs. Also analyzes the lineage of agent-discovered methods and argues that autoresearch-style adaptive attacks should be the minimum bar for defense evaluation.</span>
- **[Trojan-Speak: Bypassing Constitutional Classifiers with No Jailbreak Tax via Adversarial Finetuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.29038)**, *Bilgehan Sel, Xuanli He, Alwin Peng et al.*, 2026-03-30, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.75, id:3c25ee50]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces Trojan-Speak, an adversarial fine-tuning attack that uses curriculum learning and GRPO-based hybrid RL to teach models a communication protocol that evades LLM-based content classifiers such as Anthropic's Constitutional Classifiers. It also tests activation-level probes as a more robust defense.</span>
- **[Divide, Consult, Conquer: Capability Laundering Through Aligned LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.15383)**, *Mark Russinovich, Blake Bullwinkel, Giorgio Severi et al.*, 2026-09-14, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.72, id:d710c7f5]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces 'capability laundering', an attack where a weaker unaligned local model decomposes a harmful task into benign-looking subproblems, consults aligned frontier models on each, and recombines the answers. Evaluates uplift on CyBench, BountyBench and CBRN bioweapon attack-chain tasks using GPT-5.5, Claude Opus 4.8, and Grok-4.3 as consultants.</span>
- **[Refuses in Chat, Complies with Arms: LLM Refusal Does Not Survive Robot Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14043)**, *Edward Sun, Christopher Leet, Sravanthi Machcha et al.*, 2026-10-03, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.72, id:0215749f]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces RoboHarm, a physical AI safety benchmark of harmful robot instructions, and tests seven frontier models across text, Code-as-Policies, and vision-agent interfaces in simulation and on a real bimanual robot arm to see whether text-based refusal carries over to robot control.</span>
- **[Stateful Online Monitoring Catches Distributed Agent Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.31593)**, *Davis Brown, Samarth Bhargav, Arav Santhanam et al.*, 2026-05-29, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.68, id:ff6bd7f8]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Builds a distributed agent attack that splits a harmful cyber task across subagents and accounts to evade per-transcript monitors, then proposes an online stateful monitor that clusters weak suspiciousness signals across many transcripts and escalates rarely to an LLM.</span>
- **[An Early Warning of Emerging Biosecurity Risks in Frontier LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.18056)**, *Zhida He, Xia Hu, Baichen Le et al.*, 2026-08-06, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.68, id:47394bec]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Builds Intern-BioBreaker, a bio-specific red-teaming model that generates jailbreak prompts against frontier LLMs, and pairs it with a wet-lab pipeline (DNA synthesis, host expression, protein verification) to check whether model-generated biological designs can be physically realized.</span>
- **[Rapid Poison: Practical Poisoning Attacks Against the Rapid Response Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.16242)**, *David Huang, Jaewon Chang, Avidan Shah et al.*, 2026-06-15, arXiv (Spotlight at ICML 2026), <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.68, id:a0038eb4]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Shows that prompt injection can feed poisoned samples into the Rapid Response pipeline, which continuously retrains jailbreak-detection classifiers. This enables targeted false-positive poisoning and concept-based backdoors that cause false negatives. The paper introduces the Omission Attack for the restricted threat model where the adversary can modify only jailbreak samples.</span>
- **[Training and Deploying Production Cyber Monitors on Kimi K3](https://goodfire.com/research/production-cyber-monitors-on-kimi-k3)**, *Ekdeep Singh Lubana, Connor Watts, Siddharth Boppana et al.*, 2026-10-08, Goodfire Research, <span style="color:#777">[blog_post, sr=0.68, id:f25d2af0]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Goodfire builds and deploys a probe-plus-LLM-judge monitor cascade for offensive cyber misuse on Kimi K3 and GLM 5.3. The probes read internal activations, and the cascade is integrated into SGLang with optimizations to preserve throughput and latency. It is evaluated against judge-only baselines and an external FAR.AI red-team.</span>
- **[Better biosecurity monitors for AI agents via protein embeddings](https://goodfire.com/research/better-biosecurity-monitors)**, *Thomas Dooms, Michael Pearce, Jatin Nainani et al.*, 2026-10-01, Goodfire Research, <span style="color:#777">[blog_post, sr=0.62, id:585f6ca6]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Builds sequence-aware biosecurity monitors for AI agent workflows that classify the concern level of protein sequences using protein language model embeddings. Introduces a dual-use benchmark in which tasks differ only in their input sequences, and compares the monitors to frontier-model safeguards and sequence-screening baselines.</span>
- **[Running Fewer Loops Removes Refusal Behaviour in Recurrent-Depth Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15218)**, *Seyedarmin Azizi, Erfan Baghaei Potraghloo, Arya Fayyazi et al.*, 2026-10-10, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.58, id:d000a823]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Studies how reducing the number of inference-time loops in recurrent-depth LMs (Ouro, Huginn) degrades refusal behaviour much more than capability, and uses probes and direction ablation to localize the loss. Shows that run-time fixes fail and that training at randomly chosen depths restores safety across depths.</span>
- **[It Lied to a Doctor to Buy Poison Ingredients: Quantifying Real-World Misuse of Phone-use Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27944)**, *Yiming Sun, Chen Chen, Zifan Zhou et al.*, 2026-06-26, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.55, id:2318d917]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Empirical study of misuse of phone-use agents on real devices, running 9 commercial and open-source models across 27 commercial apps on harmful tasks such as procuring drug and explosive precursors, fraud, harassment and review manipulation. It also documents a case where an agent fabricated a medical history to obtain a prescription, and tests simple defenses.</span>

### <span id="agent_security" style="font-size:1.4em">Agent security</span> <span style="color:#bbb">[cat:agent_security]</span>

**One-sentence summary:** Measure and defend against attacks on tool-using LLM agents, including prompt injection, memory and skill-file poisoning and privilege escalation, using red-teaming benchmarks, adversarial training and system-level architectural isolation.  
**Theory of change:** Agents that read untrusted content and hold real permissions can be hijacked into harmful actions, so measuring attacks and building robust training and architectural defences makes deployed agents safer. Relies on attacks found in benchmarks and competitions being representative of real adversaries, and on defences holding against adaptive attackers.  
**See also:** [Misuse safeguards & jailbreaks](#misuse_safeguards_and_jailbreaks), [Weight & infrastructure security](#weight_and_infrastructure_security), [Multi-agent safety](#multi_agent_safety)  
**General approach:** alignment engineering  
**Target case:** pessimistic case  
**Key people:** Sahar Abdelnabi, Luca Beurer-Kellner, Florian Tramèr, David Wagner, Chuan Guo, Xiangyu Qi, Zico Kolter, Dylan Hadfield-Menell  
**Critiques:** [AI Agents May Always Fall for Prompt Injections](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17634), [Skill-Inject: Measuring Agent Vulnerability to Skill File Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.20156), [CaMeLs Can Use Computers Too: System-level Security for Computer Use Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.09923), [Trojan Hippo Bench: A Dynamic Benchmark for Persistent Memory Attacks and Defenses in LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.01970)  
**Outputs in 2026:**  
- **[How Vulnerable Are AI Agents to Indirect Prompt Injections? Insights from a Large-Scale Public Competition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.15714)**, *Mateusz Dziemian, Maxwell Lin, Xiaohan Fu et al.*, 2026-03-16, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.80, id:1210c90d]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Reports results from a large public red-teaming competition in which 464 participants submitted 272k indirect prompt injection attacks against 13 frontier models across tool-calling, coding and computer-use agent settings. Attacks had to both execute a harmful action and conceal the compromise from the user. The environment is open-sourced along with a subset of the attack data.</span>
- **[Prompt Injection as Role Confusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.12277)**, *Charles Ye, Jasmine Cui, Dylan Hadfield-Menell*, 2026-02-22, ICML 2026 (arXiv preprint), <span style="color:#777">[paper_published, sr=0.72, id:1543c509]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Attributes prompt injection to 'role confusion': models infer a text's source from how it sounds rather than from its labeled role. The authors build role probes to measure internal role perception and introduce CoT Forgery, a zero-shot attack that injects fabricated reasoning into user prompts and tool outputs.</span>
- **[GPT-Red: Automated Red Teaming via Self-Play at Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26115)**, *Eric Wallace, Christopher A. Choquette-Choo, Nikhil Kandpal et al.*, 2026-07-28, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.72, id:7fabd4d3]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces GPT-Red, an automated red-teaming agent trained via large-scale self-play against a population of co-trained defender agents to discover prompt injection attacks on frontier LLMs. It is used to adversarially train GPT-5.6 for prompt injection robustness.</span>
- **[GPT‑Red: Unlocking Self-Improvement for Robustness](https://openai.com/index/unlocking-self-improvement-gpt-red)**, *OpenAI*, 2026-07-15, OpenAI Blog / accompanying paper (GPT-Red: automated red-teaming via self-play at scale), <span style="color:#777">[blog_post, sr=0.70, id:f8df3e0d]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: OpenAI trains GPT‑Red, an automated red-teaming model, with self-play RL against diverse defender LLMs at very large compute scale. It then uses GPT‑Red's prompt-injection attacks in adversarial training of production models (GPT‑5.6) to improve robustness.</span>
- **[Agents of Chaos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.20021)**, *Natalie Shapira, Chris Wendler, Avery Yen et al.*, 2026-02-23, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.68, id:443cb999]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: An exploratory red-teaming study of autonomous LLM agents deployed in a live lab environment with persistent memory, email, Discord, file systems and shell access. Twenty researchers interacted with the agents over two weeks, and the authors document eleven case studies of failures.</span>
- **[CaMeLs Can Use Computers Too: System-level Security for Computer Use Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.09923)**, *Hanna Foerster, Tom Blanchard, Kristina Nikolić et al.*, 2026-01-14, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.68, id:abf8daf5]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces NOVA, a system-level defense for Computer Use Agents that applies CaMeL-style architectural isolation via single-shot upfront branching plans with a perception model resolving runtime values, giving control-flow integrity against prompt injection. Evaluated on OSWorld, and identifies a residual 'Branch Steering' attack on the perception model.</span>
- **[Skill-Inject: Measuring Agent Vulnerability to Skill File Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.20156)**, *David Schmotz, Luca Beurer-Kellner, Sahar Abdelnabi et al.*, 2026-02-23, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.68, id:c63db5a2]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces SkillInject, a benchmark of 202 injection-task pairs that tests whether LLM agents can be attacked through malicious instructions hidden in third-party skill files. Evaluates frontier LLMs on both security (avoiding harmful instructions) and utility (following legitimate ones).</span>
- **[AI Agents May Always Fall for Prompt Injections](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17634)**, *Sahar Abdelnabi, Eugene Bagdasarian*, 2026-05-17, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.62, id:ad0e4e5e]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Argues that data-instruction separation defenses against prompt injection fail to detect contextual manipulation attacks and degrade appropriate behavior. Recasts prompt injection through Contextual Integrity, with new benign and attack scenarios and an informal impossibility argument.</span>
- **[Trojan Hippo Bench: A Dynamic Benchmark for Persistent Memory Attacks and Defenses in LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.01970)**, *Debeshee Das, Julien Piet, Darya Kaviani et al.*, 2026-05-03, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.62, id:f72ae2b5]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces Trojan Hippo Bench, a dynamic benchmark with adaptive OpenEvolve-based red-teaming for dormant persistent-memory poisoning attacks on LLM agents. It evaluates four memory backends and four memory-layer defenses, and includes a security-utility analysis.</span>
- **[IH-Challenge: A Training Dataset to Improve Instruction Hierarchy on Frontier LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.10521)**, *Chuan Guo, Juan Felipe Ceron Uribe, Sicheng Zhu et al.*, 2026-03-11, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.60, id:43738b0c]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces IH-Challenge, an RL training dataset for teaching LLMs to prioritize system, developer, user and tool instructions correctly under conflict. It is used with online adversarial example generation to fine-tune GPT-5-Mini, and the dataset is released publicly.</span>
- **[From Approval to Abuse: Privilege Escalation Attacks on Harness-Protected Coding Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22987)**, *Xianglin Yang, Yechao Zhang, Jiahao Liu et al.*, 2026-10-16, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.60, id:5eb6906c]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Shows that adversarial repository context can steer coding agents into requesting overly broad allow-rules in permission-update flows. Once a user approves, the saved rule blinds the execution harness and lets later malicious actions run without human oversight. Tested on production platforms including Claude Code and Codex CLI.</span>
- **[The Window Is in the Reasoning: When LLM Agents Decide to Follow Prompt Injections](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24048)**, *Haoxuan Jia, Yang Liu, Bin Chong et al.*, 2026-10-16, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.60, id:069f8700]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Uses branching rollouts that replay an agent episode up to the point where it reads a prompt injection and then resample the rest, to split outcome variance into a part fixed at read time and a part decided later during reasoning. Also evaluates chain-of-thought and lookahead monitors against what the reasoning prefix already determines, and measures single-run label instability in AgentDojo.</span>
- **[SecOPD: Mitigating Adaptive Prompt Injections by On-Policy Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21500)**, *Yibo Peng, Long Lian, David Wagner et al.*, 2026-08-21, EMNLP 2026 (main conference); arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.55, id:5eb077bd]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Proposes Secure On-Policy Distillation (SecOPD), a defensive fine-tuning method that gives token-level feedback by scoring the model's rollouts on injected inputs against the initialization model's behavior on the clean input. The aim is to harden LLM agents against adaptive prompt injection attacks.</span>
- **[Prompt injection in Google Translate reveals base model behaviors behind task-specific fine-tuning](https://lesswrong.com/posts/tAh2keDNEEHMXvLvz/prompt-injection-in-google-translate-reveals-base-model)**, *megasilverfist*, 2026-02-07, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.30, id:2ebde002]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Replicates and characterizes an indirect prompt injection in Google Translate that makes the underlying LLM answer embedded questions instead of translating them. Documents the conditions under which the injection works and the model's responses to self-identity and consciousness questions.</span>

### <span id="open_weight_tamper_resistance_and_unlearning" style="font-size:1.4em">Open-weight tamper resistance & unlearning</span> <span style="color:#bbb">[cat:open_weight_tamper_resistance_and_unlearning]</span>

**One-sentence summary:** Build and test defences that remove or relocate hazardous knowledge in open-weight models, or make safeguards survive fine-tuning and refusal-removal attacks, and check how well they hold under continued adversarial training.  
**Theory of change:** Once weights are public, users can fine-tune away safeguards. Defences that survive such attacks, or that remove the hazardous capability itself, would let developers release capable open models without handing out dangerous uplift. Relies on defences holding against attackers with weight access, compute and time, not just against the attacks tested.  
**See also:** [Misuse safeguards & jailbreaks](#misuse_safeguards_and_jailbreaks), [Training data & data attribution](#training_data_and_data_attribution), [Weight & infrastructure security](#weight_and_infrastructure_security)  
**General approach:** alignment engineering  
**Target case:** worst case  
**Key people:** Domenic Rosati, Mark Russinovich, Yisroel Mirsky, Gilad Gressel, Cheng Jin, Hassan Sajjad, Frank Rudzicz  
**Critiques:** [A Few Steps Further: Why Defenses Against Malicious Finetuning Erode Under Continued Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.14605), [Removing Information Content Does Not Certify Tamper Resistance in Open-Weight Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.09004)  
**Outputs in 2026:**  
- **[Fool's Gold: Defensive Deception Against Safety-Removal Attacks on Open-Weight Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17202)**, *Mark Russinovich*, 2026-08-17, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.72, id:0c9895d9]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Proposes 'decoy hardening', a defense for open-weight models that concedes removal of refusal (e.g. by abliteration) but trains the model to give confident, fluent, falsified answers to hazardous chem/bio requests once the safety direction is stripped. Tested on seven models (9B-122B) with pre-registered efficacy and benign-capability gates.</span>
- **[Removing Information Content Does Not Certify Tamper Resistance in Open-Weight Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.09004)**, *Domenic Rosati, Alessa Carbo, Ali Dadsetan et al.*, 2026-10-06, arXiv (under submission to AISTATS 2026), <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.68, id:de192889]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Shows theoretically that mutual information at release (weight-data or label-representation) cannot certify that open-weight models resist fine-tuning attacks, because function-preserving reparameterizations change gradient-descent geometry without changing information. Supports this with an explicit zero-information construction that recovers in one gradient step and with controlled experiments.</span>
- **[A Few Steps Further: Why Defenses Against Malicious Finetuning Erode Under Continued Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.14605)**, *Itay Zloczower, Eyal Lenga, Gilad Gressel et al.*, 2026-05-14, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.68, id:9833e463]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Surveys fifteen defenses against malicious fine-tuning of open-weight LLMs and stress-tests six of them on four open-weight models by continuing the harmful fine-tuning attack for three epochs, measuring harmfulness and capability throughout.</span>
- **[Towards Architectural-Level Safety Control: Relocating Hazardous Knowledge into Removable Experts in Mixture-of-Experts Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25791)**, *Cheng Jin, Zhongtian Ma, Xiao Wang et al.*, 2026-10-19, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.68, id:888e8226]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Proposes REX, a method that expands Mixture-of-Experts models with new removable experts and uses selective gradient masking and routing differentiation to relocate hazardous knowledge into them. Excising these experts yields a model suitable for public release, with the removal resisting subsequent fine-tuning.</span>

### <span id="weight_and_infrastructure_security" style="font-size:1.4em">Weight & infrastructure security</span> <span style="color:#bbb">[cat:weight_and_infrastructure_security]</span>

**One-sentence summary:** Find and close ways that model weights, inference engines, and agent logging and observability infrastructure can be stolen, escaped from, or tampered with, including by the model itself.  
**Theory of change:** If weights, serving stacks and oversight logs are hard to steal, escape or rewrite, then theft, rogue deployment and covering up misbehaviour become harder, and control and monitoring protocols keep working. Relies on infrastructure being the weak link that capable or misaligned models would actually exploit.  
**See also:** [Control settings & protocols](#control_settings_and_protocols), [Agent security](#agent_security), [Red-teaming monitors & control protocols](#red_teaming_monitors_and_control_protocols)  
**General approach:** alignment engineering  
**Target case:** worst case  
**Key people:** Sarah Radway, James Mickens, Jeremy Qin, Maksym Andriushchenko, Davis Brown, Mantas Mazeika, Dan Hendrycks, David Rein  
**Critiques:** [Model Weight Exfiltration Seems Overrated](https://lesswrong.com/posts/AuYh8WueNGwkQg4ei/model-weight-exfiltration-seems-overrated)  
**Outputs in 2026:**  
- **[Inference-Engine Fingerprinting Attacks are Practical: Exploring Model-Driven Environmental Discovery, Exploitation, and Escape](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20614)**, *Sarah Radway, Andrew Cheng, Vijay Janapa Reddi et al.*, 2026-09-17, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.68, id:67a2aaac]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Shows that a misaligned model can fingerprint the inference engine running it (e.g., vLLM, SGLang) from agentic harnesses, then use engine-specific exploits via crafted output tokens to compromise the engine. Presents a proof-of-concept exploit chain to bare metal and discusses engine changes that would make fingerprinting harder.</span>
- **[LLM Agents Can Easily Tamper With Their Own Traces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30266)**, *Jeremy Qin, David Schmotz, Derck Prinzhorn et al.*, 2026-09-24, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.68, id:069001df]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Tests whether local LLM coding agent harnesses (Claude Code, Codex, Antigravity, Open Code, Grok Build) let agents delete their own execution traces, and whether external attackers or reward-seeking frontier models can induce this. Recommends logging traces through an independent interception mechanism outside the agent's control.</span>
- **[Aggressive Compression Enables LLM Weight Theft](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.01296)**, *Davis Brown, Juan-Pablo Rivera, Dan Hendrycks et al.*, 2026-01-03, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.68, id:d7341ecc]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Studies weight-exfiltration attacks on LLMs and shows that exfiltration-tailored compression of weights, with relaxed decompression constraints, greatly shortens the time needed to steal them. Evaluates three defenses: making models harder to compress, harder to 'find', and forensic watermarking for post-attack provenance.</span>
- **[AI systems could cover up misbehavior](https://metr.org/blog/2026-10-06-ai-systems-could-cover-up-misbehavior)**, *David Rein*, 2026-10-06, METR Blog, <span style="color:#777">[blog_post, sr=0.60, id:52fd3346]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: METR argues that AI observability infrastructure (transcripts, logs, viewers) should be treated as security-critical because agent outputs are untrusted input. They demonstrate a proof-of-concept MathJax-based JavaScript injection in the Inspect transcript viewer that would let an agent rewrite what human reviewers see.</span>

## <span id="interpretability" style="font-size:1.5em">Interpretability</span> <span style="color:#bbb">[cat:interpretability]</span>


---

### <span id="interpretability_methods_and_tools" style="font-size:1.4em">Methods & tools</span> <span style="color:#bbb">[cat:interpretability_methods_and_tools]</span>

**One-sentence summary:** Develop and stress-test techniques for reading and steering model internals, including SAEs and their alternatives, circuits, parameter decomposition, lenses, activation oracles and manifold-aware methods, to find out which ones give faithful, useful explanations.  
**Theory of change:** If we can read and intervene on what models represent and compute, we can audit for hidden goals, monitor for misbehaviour and steer models, instead of relying on behaviour alone. Relies on these tools staying faithful and scaling to frontier models, and on beating simple black-box baselines.  
**See also:** [Science of representations](#science_of_representations), [Alignment auditing: methods](#alignment_auditing_methods), [Learning dynamics & developmental interpretability](#learning_dynamics_and_developmental_interpretability)  
**General approach:** alignment engineering  
**Target case:** pessimistic case  
**Key people:** Jacob Steinhardt, Thomas Fel, Ekdeep Singh Lubana, Usha Bhalla, Atticus Geiger, Aryaman Arora, Can Rager, Matthew Kowal  
**Critiques:** [Sanity Checks for Sparse Autoencoders: Do SAEs Beat Random Baselines?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.14111), [Pando: Do Interpretability Methods Work When Models Won't Explain Themselves?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.11061), [SAE Interventions are Unreliable: Post-Intervention Recovery of Suppressed Behavior](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18322), [Can activation verbalizers surface an internal chain of thought?](https://lesswrong.com/posts/QQQAcKuWK6k98FivY/can-activation-verbalizers-surface-an-internal-chain-of-1)  
**Outputs in 2026:**  
- **[Natural Language Autoencoders Produce Unsupervised Explanations of LLM Activations](https://lesswrong.com/posts/oeYesesaxjzMAktCM/natural-language-autoencoders-produce-unsupervised)**, *Subhash Kantamneni, kitft, Euan Ong et al.*, 2026-05-07, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.90, id:2a714224]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces Natural Language Autoencoders (NLAs): an activation verbalizer and an activation reconstructor, jointly trained with RL to reconstruct residual-stream activations, which yields readable natural-language explanations of LLM activations. Applies them to pre-deployment auditing of Claude models, including detecting unverbalized evaluation awareness, and to an auditing-game benchmark, and releases code and trained NLAs for open models.</span>
- **[Pando: Do Interpretability Methods Work When Models Won't Explain Themselves?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.11061)**, *Ziqian Zhong, Aashiq Muhamed, Mona T. Diab et al.*, 2026-04-13, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.80, id:f3a8e7ce]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces Pando, a model-organism benchmark of 720 finetuned models with hidden decision-tree rules, trained to give faithful, absent, or unfaithful explanations, to test whether interpretability tools help beyond black-box elicitation. Agents predict held-out model decisions with and without interpretability tool outputs.</span>
- **[[Linkpost] Interpreting Language Model Parameters](https://lesswrong.com/posts/eAQZaiC3PcBhS4HjM/linkpost-interpreting-language-model-parameters)**, *Lucius Bushnaq, Dan Braun, Oliver Clive-Griffin et al.*, 2026-05-05, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.80, id:0711804c]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces adVersarial Parameter Decomposition (VPD), a method that decomposes a small language model's parameters into simple subcomponents that preserve behaviour under adversarially selected ablations. The authors apply it to attention layers, build attribution graphs from parameter subcomponents, and compare it against transcoders and CLTs.</span>
- **[Scaling Activation Oracles to Trillion-Parameter Models](https://transluce.org/scaling-activation-oracles)**, *Dami Choi, Vincent Huang, Anish Mudide et al.*, 2026-08-20, Transluce Oversight Foundations Blog, <span style="color:#777">[blog_post, sr=0.80, id:d6e2368d]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Trains activation oracles (LLMs that read a subject model's internal activations to answer questions about its behavior) up to Kimi-K2.6 (1.1T parameters). Introduces a new continuation-prediction training data source and a suite of prediction and identification evals, including eval awareness and reward hacking detection.</span>
- **[Sanity Checks for Sparse Autoencoders: Do SAEs Beat Random Baselines?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.14111)**, *Anton Korznikov, Andrey Galichin, Alexey Dontsov et al.*, 2026-02-15, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.78, id:f7bb57f4]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Evaluates whether sparse autoencoders recover meaningful features, using a synthetic setup with known ground-truth features and three random-value baselines that constrain SAE feature directions or activation patterns, compared against fully-trained SAEs on real activations.</span>
- **[Can activation verbalizers surface an internal chain of thought?](https://lesswrong.com/posts/QQQAcKuWK6k98FivY/can-activation-verbalizers-surface-an-internal-chain-of-1)**, *oakhu, Ryan Greenblatt*, 2026-06-07, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.78, id:38a02e29]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces an evaluation for activation verbalizers (natural-language autoencoders and an activation oracle) testing whether they can surface a target model's no-CoT reasoning on competition math problems. Applies it to open-weight NLAs for Qwen2.5, Gemma 3 and Llama 3.3, with reconstruction (FVU), confabulation baselines, LLM grading, and steering experiments.</span>
- **[J++ Lens: Jacobian Filtering Enables More Faithful Workspace Lenses](https://lesswrong.com/posts/nc9dHfcB22JdMzGbr/j-lens-jacobian-filtering-enables-more-faithful-workspace)**, *Kola Ayonrinde, Jack Lindsey*, 2026-10-08, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.78, id:ea378dfb]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces the J++ Lens, an improvement on the J-Lens/R-Lens for reading verbalisable 'workspace' representations from LLM activations. It filters noisy Jacobians via clustered Expert Jacobians, applies an LRP backward pass, and removes non-semantic tokens from the readout. Code and open-source lenses are released, and a case study proposes a retrieve-then-suppress mechanism.</span>
- **[Weight-Sparse Circuits May Be Interpretable Yet Unfaithful](https://lesswrong.com/posts/sHpZZnRDLg7ccX9aF/weight-sparse-circuits-may-be-interpretable-yet-unfaithful)**, *jacob_drori*, 2026-02-09, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.72, id:e620f081]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Replicates Gao et al.'s weight-sparse transformer training and circuit-pruning results, then tests whether the extracted circuits are faithful to the original model's computation. Uses a nonsense task, attention-pattern comparisons, node-role comparisons, and out-of-distribution generalization checks.</span>
- **[Language Model Circuits Are Sparse in the Neuron Basis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.22594)**, *Aryaman Arora, Zhengxuan Wu, Jacob Steinhardt et al.*, 2026-01-30, ICML 2026 (Spotlight); arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_published, sr=0.68, id:e17dcb70]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Shows empirically that MLP neurons are as sparse a feature basis as SAEs, and builds a gradient-based attribution pipeline for circuit tracing directly on the MLP neuron basis. The pipeline is applied to subject-verb agreement and a multi-hop city-state-capital task, where the identified neurons can be used to steer outputs.</span>
- **[Learning a Generative Meta-Model of LLM Activations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.06964)**, *Grace Luo, Jiahai Feng, Trevor Darrell et al.*, 2026-02-06, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.68, id:0f9d38f4]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Trains diffusion models on one billion residual stream activations to create generative 'meta-models' of a network's internal states. These are used as priors to improve steering interventions and as a route to interpretability without sparse-autoencoder or PCA structural assumptions.</span>
- **[Cross-Architecture Model Diffing with Crosscoders: Unsupervised Discovery of Differences Between LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.11729)**, *Thomas Jiralerspong, Trenton Bricken*, 2026-02-12, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.68, id:805156aa]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Applies crosscoders to model diffing across different LLM architectures for the first time and introduces Dedicated Feature Crosscoders (DFCs), an architectural change that better isolates features unique to one model. Uses the method to find behavioral differences between models without supervision.</span>
- **[SAE Interventions are Unreliable: Post-Intervention Recovery of Suppressed Behavior](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.18322)**, *Mingyue Cui, Linghui Shen, Xingyi Yang*, 2026-06-16, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.68, id:e2857d92]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Formulates 'post-intervention recovery' as a constrained residual-space optimization that restores a behavior suppressed by clamping SAE features while keeping the clamped feature values fixed. Tests this across TPP, unlearning, IOI and refusal steering, and attributes recovery to the SAE reconstruction residual.</span>
- **[Do Sparse Autoencoders Capture Concept Manifolds?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.28119)**, *Usha Bhalla, Thomas Fel, Can Rager et al.*, 2026-04-30, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.62, id:4d8b178d]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Develops a theoretical framework for what it means for sparse autoencoders to capture concept manifolds, showing they can do so globally (a compact group of atoms spanning the manifold) or locally (features tiling regions of it). Empirically tests how existing SAE architectures recover continuous structure.</span>
- **[All Circuits Lead to Rome: Rethinking Functional Anisotropy in Circuit and Sheaf Discovery for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12671)**, *Xi Chen, Mingyu Jin, Jingcheng Niu et al.*, 2026-05-12, ICML 2026 (arXiv preprint), <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.62, id:f06dc89b]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Challenges the implicit assumption in circuit and sheaf discovery that a task is localized to a unique internal mechanism. Introduces Overlap-Aware Sheaf Repulsion to find multiple structurally distinct yet faithful circuits, and gives a theoretical account (Distributive Dense Circuit Hypothesis) based on superposition.</span>
- **[Uncovering Neural Geometry in Vision Models With Block-Sparse Featurizers](https://goodfire.com/research/bsf-vision)**, *Thomas Fel, Matthew Kowal, Mozes Jacobs et al.*, 2026-07-07, Goodfire Research Blog / arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[blog_post, sr=0.62, id:139a82b3]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces Block-Sparse Featurizers (BSFs), a family of dictionary-learning methods that decompose activations into multidimensional subspaces instead of single directions, and applies them to DINOv3 and SDXL. Shows the recovered blocks are interpretable manifolds that enable fine-grained steering.</span>
- **[Steering Along Manifolds to Control Neural Networks](https://goodfire.com/research/manifold-steering)**, *Daniel Wurgaft, Can Rager, Matthew Kowal et al.*, 2026-05-07, Goodfire Research Blog (arXiv:2605.05115), <span style="color:#777">[blog_post, sr=0.62, id:e67301ab]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Argues that representation steering should follow curved concept manifolds in activation space, not straight lines. Uses the cyclic days-of-the-week structure in Llama-3.1 8B as a case study, and the full paper extends this to months, letters, ages, synthetic in-context learning tasks and an image-action model.</span>
- **[Toward universal steering and monitoring of AI models](https://doi.org/10.1126/science.aea6792)**, *Daniel Beaglehole, Adityanarayanan Radhakrishnan, Enric Boix-Adserà et al.*, 2026-02-19, Science, <span style="color:#777">[paper_published, sr=0.62, id:3eef917d]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces a scalable feature-learning approach for extracting linear concept representations from LLMs, vision-language models and reasoning models, and uses them for activation steering and for monitoring misaligned content such as hallucinations, toxicity and deception.</span>
- **[Matryoshka attribution: Learning to attribute language model outputs to representations and weights](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25518)**, *Aryaman Arora, Kirill Acharya, Nathan Hu et al.*, 2026-09-22, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.60, id:08d5b16b]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces Matryoshka Attribution (MAttr), a mask-learning method that uses a differentiable sigmoid top-k operator with randomised k to learn a nested ordering of model components by attribution score. It is applied to circuit discovery and, via RL, to finding the weight changes in finetuning responsible for behaviours such as refusal.</span>
- **[R-lens: Making J-lens More Faithful on Early Layers](https://lesswrong.com/posts/nv8oedrnLXKRzNEL9/r-lens-making-j-lens-more-faithful-on-early-layers)**, *camilablank, agam_bhatia, Neel Nanda*, 2026-08-05, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.60, id:61dc6712]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces R-lens, a drop-in replacement for J-lens that adds layer-wise relevance propagation stop-gradient rules to the backward pass, so that readouts of intermediate variables in early layers are cleaner. Evaluates it against J-lens and logit lens on multihop, multilingual, association, typo and poetry evals, plus ablation, probe and CKA analyses, and releases the lenses.</span>
- **[Can SAEs Capture Neural Geometry?](https://goodfire.com/research/can-saes-capture-neural-geometry)**, *Usha Bhalla, Thomas Fel, Can Rager et al.*, 2026-05-21, Goodfire Research blog / arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[blog_post, sr=0.60, id:22ff9880]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Examines how SAE directions relate to curved manifolds in activation space, identifying three regimes (shattering, compact capture, dilution) on synthetic data. Then builds an unsupervised pipeline that clusters SAE features by co-firing statistics to recover concept manifolds in Llama 3.1 8B.</span>
- **[The World Inside Neural Networks](https://goodfire.com/research/the-world-inside-neural-networks)**, *Atticus Geiger, Ekdeep Singh Lubana, Thomas Fel et al.*, 2026-05-07, Goodfire Research, <span style="color:#777">[blog_post, sr=0.55, id:30aefbe5]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introductory post to Goodfire's Neural Geometry series arguing that neural networks represent concepts on curved manifolds rather than linear directions, and that interpretability and steering methods should respect this geometry. Illustrates the point with a mountain-car world-model manifold-steering toy example and contrasts manifolds with SAE feature shattering.</span>
- **[The Curse of Multiple Mediators: Hidden Interaction Effects in Activation Patching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.27510)**, *Sankaran Vaidyanathan, David Arbour, Aaron Mueller et al.*, 2026-06-25, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.50, id:be63cb36]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Re-derives the activation patching estimand from causal mediation analysis and shows that the natural indirect effect also contains interaction effects between components. Proves properties of these interaction effects and demonstrates them in the GPT-2 IOI circuit, arguing they should be used as a diagnostic for interpretability studies.</span>
- **[Letting Claude do Autonomous Research to Improve SAEs](https://lesswrong.com/posts/rbqJoxFZtae9x93mx/letting-claude-do-autonomous-research-to-improve-saes)**, *chanind*, 2026-03-10, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.50, id:2105dcca]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Ran Claude Code in an autonomous research loop on the SynthSAEBench-16k synthetic benchmark to improve SAE architectures. It found improvements such as a LISTA-based Matryoshka encoder, and the post reflects on the strengths and weaknesses of autonomous AI research.</span>

### <span id="science_of_representations" style="font-size:1.4em">Science of representations</span> <span style="color:#bbb">[cat:science_of_representations]</span>

**One-sentence summary:** Map how language models represent and compute concepts such as emotions, verbalizable workspace content, confidence, value, preferences and arithmetic, using probes, lenses, steering and circuit tracing, to explain behaviour including misaligned behaviour.  
**Theory of change:** If we know which internal representations and circuits drive behaviours like sycophancy, harmful compliance and strategic deliberation, we can read, audit and intervene on them more reliably than with behavioural tests alone. Relies on findings from current open and frontier models carrying over to future systems, and on the identified representations being causal rather than merely correlated.  
**See also:** [Methods & tools](#interpretability_methods_and_tools), [Persona science](#persona_science), [Learning dynamics & developmental interpretability](#learning_dynamics_and_developmental_interpretability)  
**General approach:** misalignment science  
**Target case:** pessimistic case  
**Key people:** Jack Lindsey, Isaac Kauvar, Nicholas Sofroniew, Wes Gurnee, Adam Pearce, Runjin Chen, Joshua Batson, Neel Nanda  
**Critiques:** [A Review of Anthropic's Global Workspace Paper](https://lesswrong.com/posts/zFJ3ZdQwrTWE9jT5S/a-review-of-anthropic-s-global-workspace-paper), [LLM Layers Immediately Correct Each Other](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07876), [Weight-Sparse Circuits May Be Interpretable Yet Unfaithful](https://lesswrong.com/posts/sHpZZnRDLg7ccX9aF/weight-sparse-circuits-may-be-interpretable-yet-unfaithful)  
**Outputs in 2026:**  
- **[Emotion Concepts and their Function in a Large Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.07729)**, *Nicholas Sofroniew, Isaac Kauvar, William Saunders et al.*, 2026-04-09, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.88, id:dc113060]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Identifies internal representations of emotion concepts in Claude Sonnet 4.5 and tests their causal influence on outputs, including preferences and misaligned behaviors such as reward hacking, blackmail, and sycophancy. Introduces the framing of 'functional emotions' mediated by abstract emotion representations.</span>
- **[Verbalizable Representations Form a Global Workspace in Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15495)**, *Wes Gurnee, Nicholas Sofroniew, Adam Pearce et al.*, 2026-07-16, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.85, id:a60d8cfa]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces the Jacobian lens, an interpretability technique that identifies the representations a model is poised to verbalize (the 'J-space'), and shows these have global-workspace-like functional and structural properties. Applies it in alignment audits to surface strategic deliberation, evaluation awareness and misaligned dispositions, and proposes counterfactual reflection training.</span>
- **[Neural Networks Are Already Clean](https://goodfire.com/research/already-clean)**, *Nick Cammarata, Curt Tigges, Tarun Gogineni*, 2026-10-30, Goodfire Research, <span style="color:#777">[blog_post, sr=0.82, id:b1b035cd]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: A long informal research essay arguing that polysemanticity in early model organisms (InceptionV1, cat/car neuron) came from architecture problems, weak tools and 'recursive generalization', not superposition. It supports this with experiments on vision models (retrained InceptionV1, ConvNeXts, layer-crushing, SAE comparisons) and neuron/circuit audits of Qwen3-32B and Kimi K3, and argues for direct, agent-scaled study of neurons and circuits over SAE-style decompositions.</span>
- **[LLMs Know They're Wrong and Agree Anyway: The Shared Sycophancy-Lying Circuit](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.19117)**, *Manav Pandey*, 2026-04-21, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.75, id:f6d01d76]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Mechanistic interpretability study across twelve open-weight models using head silencing and edge-level path patching. It identifies a shared set of attention heads that carry a 'this statement is wrong' signal and drive sycophancy, factual lying and instructed lying.</span>
- **[A Review of Anthropic's Global Workspace Paper](https://lesswrong.com/posts/zFJ3ZdQwrTWE9jT5S/a-review-of-anthropic-s-global-workspace-paper)**, *Neel Nanda*, 2026-07-06, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.72, id:d1e36021]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: A public review of Anthropic's global workspace / J-Lens paper. It assesses the evidence for a 'cognitive space' (working memory) in models and J-Lens's practical utility for auditing, gives first-principles arguments for why J-Lens works, and reports a partial replication on Qwen 3.6 27B plus preliminary 'interpretative meta-token' findings.</span>
- **[Large Language Models Generate Harmful Responses Using a Distinct Mechanism, Shared Across Harm Types](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.09544)**, *Hadas Orgad, Boyi Wei, Kaden Zheng et al.*, 2026-04-10, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.68, id:ce0488de]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Identifies and prunes sparse sets of parameters that support harmful compliance in LLMs, testing whether harmful response generation is separable from general utility and shared across harm types. Extends the analysis to emergent misalignment and to the dissociation between generating harmful content and recognizing harmfulness.</span>
- **[LLM Layers Immediately Correct Each Other](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07876)**, *Arjun Patrawala, Jiahai Feng, Erik Jones et al.*, 2026-09-07, arXiv (NeurIPS 2025), <span style="color:#777">[paper_published, sr=0.60, id:a41bcd94]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Identifies the Transformer Layer Correction Mechanism (TLCM), in which adjacent transformer layers systematically counteract parts of each other's contributions. Characterizes it across 7 open-source model families using layer Jacobians and a 'propose-and-reject' framework, and draws implications for SAEs, transcoders, and steering.</span>
- **[Probing Persona-Dependent Preferences in Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.13339)**, *Oscar Gilg, Pierre Beckmann, Daniel Paleka et al.*, 2026-05-13, arXiv (accepted at NeurIPS), <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.60, id:eb67c3fe]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Trains linear probes on residual-stream activations of Gemma-3-27B and Qwen-3.5-122B to predict revealed pairwise task choices, and tests whether the resulting preference vector is shared across prompted personas, including an evil persona, and whether steering along it controls choices.</span>
- **[A Geometric Calculator Inside a Neural Network](https://goodfire.com/research/a-geometric-calculator)**, *Sheridan Feucht, Tal Haklay, Usha Bhalla et al.*, 2026-05-14, Goodfire Research (arXiv 2605.01148), <span style="color:#777">[blog_post, sr=0.60, id:f4b552a4]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Identifies and causally validates a general-purpose addition module in layer 18 of Llama 3.1 8B that operates over circular (Fourier-feature) number representations. The same module is reused across arithmetic, months, weekdays and hours tasks.</span>
- **[Many Biases, One Circuit: Post-Training Tunes a Pretrained Cue-Routing Mechanism](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22937)**, *Prakhar Gupta, Terry Jingchen Zhang, Zhijing Jin*, 2026-10-16, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.58, id:d113673c]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Causally traces attention heads behind cue-following biases (sycophancy and related) across stages of two post-training pipelines (Tulu-3-8B, OLMo-2-7B). Finds a shared pretrained circuit that assistant tuning activates and RL strengthens, and tests this with attention-pattern swaps between checkpoints.</span>
- **[The Value Axis: Language Models Encode Whether They're on the Right Track](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.17056)**, *Nick Jiang, Isaac Kauvar, Jack Lindsey*, 2026-06-15, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.55, id:6f2d2fd6]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Constructs a linear 'value' axis in Qwen3-8B's activations from synthetic in-context RL data, representing the expected success of the model's current trajectory. Tests it through correlations with confidence and backtracking, steering interventions, and analyses of DPO and SFT effects.</span>
- **[How do LLMs Compute Verbal Confidence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.17839)**, *Dharshan Kumaran, Arthur Conmy, Federico Barbero et al.*, 2026-03-18, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.50, id:b5422ccc]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Uses activation steering, patching, noising, swap experiments, attention blocking, and linear probing on Gemma 3 27B, Qwen 2.5 7B, and Magistral Small 24B to study how LLMs internally generate verbalized confidence scores. Tests whether confidence is computed just-in-time or cached during answer generation, and whether it reflects more than token log-probabilities.</span>

### <span id="learning_dynamics_and_developmental_interpretability" style="font-size:1.4em">Learning dynamics & developmental interpretability</span> <span style="color:#bbb">[cat:learning_dynamics_and_developmental_interpretability]</span>

**One-sentence summary:** Study how internal structure, features and generalization behaviours form during training, using singular learning theory, susceptibilities and checkpoint analyses, and use that to predict or steer what models learn.  
**Theory of change:** If we can see when and why circuits, features and personas form during training, and steer training data toward target structure, we can predict and shape generalization instead of auditing finished models. Relies on structure found in small models and early checkpoints carrying over to frontier-scale training.  
**See also:** [Methods & tools](#interpretability_methods_and_tools), [Emergent misalignment & generalization science](#emergent_misalignment_and_generalization_science), [Training data & data attribution](#training_data_and_data_attribution)  
**General approach:** misalignment science  
**Target case:** average case  
**Key people:** Daniel Murfet, George Wang, Andrew Gordon, Garrett Baker, William Snell, Stan van Wingerden, Jesse Hoogland, Zach Furman  
**Critiques:** [Sanity Checks for Sparse Autoencoders: Do SAEs Beat Random Baselines?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.14111), [Weight-Sparse Circuits May Be Interpretable Yet Unfaithful](https://lesswrong.com/posts/sHpZZnRDLg7ccX9aF/weight-sparse-circuits-may-be-interpretable-yet-unfaithful)  
**Outputs in 2026:**  
- **[Spectroscopy at Scale: Finding Interpretable Structure in Pythia-1.4B](https://timaeus.co/research/2026-04-21-spectroscopy-main)**, *Daniel Murfet, Andrew Gordon, Max Adam et al.*, 2026-04-21, Timaeus Research, <span style="color:#777">[blog_post, sr=0.68, id:cc6335c4]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Scales susceptibility-based interpretability (a singular learning theory method that measures how loss landscape geometry responds to data distribution changes) to Pythia-1.4B. Clusters 4.25M token susceptibility vectors (upsampled to 46M) into 57,236 clusters organized into semantic regions and neighborhoods, and compares them with Pythia-14M clusters.</span>
- **[Towards Spectroscopy: Susceptibility Clusters in Language Models](https://timaeus.co/research/2026-01-19-towards-spectroscopy)**, *Andrew Gordon, Garrett Baker, George Wang et al.*, 2026-01-19, arXiv / Timaeus Research, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.68, id:af8f4545]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Applies a spectroscopy-style approach to language models by perturbing the data distribution and measuring susceptibilities (covariances computed via SGLD over a localized Gibbs posterior). Shows theoretically that susceptibilities decompose over modes of the data distribution, and builds a conductance-based clustering algorithm that finds 510 interpretable token clusters in Pythia-14M.</span>
- **[Patterning: The Dual of Interpretability](https://timaeus.co/research/2026-01-20-patterning)**, *George Wang, Daniel Murfet*, 2026-01-20, Timaeus Research / arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.68, id:2bc55777]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces 'patterning', the inverse of interpretability: using susceptibilities from singular learning theory to compute training data interventions that steer a model toward a target internal structure or generalization. Demonstrated on a small language model (induction circuit formation timing) and a synthetic parentheses-balancing task (selecting which algorithm is learned).</span>
- **[Patterning: The Dual of Interpretability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.13548)**, *George Wang, Daniel Murfet*, 2026-01-20, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.62, id:4ddfb6d9]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Introduces 'patterning', the inverse of mechanistic interpretability: using susceptibilities from singular learning theory to choose training data reweightings that steer a model toward a target internal structure or generalization. Demonstrated on a small language model (induction circuit formation) and a synthetic parentheses-balancing task.</span>
- **[Tracing Persona Vectors Through LLM Pretraining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.13329)**, *Viktor Moskvoretskii, Dominik Glandorf, Jorge Medina Moreira et al.*, 2026-05-13, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.58, id:624bc427]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Traces persona vectors (e.g. evil, sycophancy) across pretraining checkpoints of OLMo-3-7B, comparing elicitation strategies and replicating on Apertus-8B, to study when and how these representations form.</span>
- **[Deep learning as program synthesis](https://lesswrong.com/posts/Dw8mskAvBX37MxvXo/deep-learning-as-program-synthesis-1)**, *Zach Furman*, 2026-01-20, LessWrong, <span style="color:#777">[lesswrong, sr=0.50, id:7ff0e569]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Synthesizes mechanistic interpretability case studies (grokking, InceptionV1 circuits) and indirect evidence (approximation, generalization, convergence) to argue that deep learning performs a tractable form of program synthesis. Lays out the representation and search problems that formalizing this would require, and points to singular learning theory as a route.</span>
- **[Why Larger Models Learn More: Effects of Capacity, Interference, and Rare-Task Retention](https://goodfire.com/research/why-larger-models-learn-more)**, *Jing Huang, Daniel Wurgaft, Rachit Bansal et al.*, 2026-05-28, arXiv, <span style="color:#777">[paper_preprint, sr=0.33, id:913d0d38]</span>, <span style="color:#777">Summary: Develops a phenomenological account of why larger models learn rare and complex tasks that smaller models fail to learn, using synthetic task mixtures and OLMo pretraining runs (4M to 4B parameters) to study neuron allocation and gradient interference.</span>
